# Integration Test: /kandalf resume

---

## TC-RESUME-01: Resume from Valid Checkpoint

**Setup**: `memory.md` with task T-002 status=`interrupted`, checkpoint `T-002-step2-S-001` exists in `.kandalf/checkpoints/`, owner was `S-001` (not current session).

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf resume` in a new session. Choose "resume" for T-002.

**Expected**:
- T-002 flagged as interrupted (stale lock: owner S-001 ≠ current session)
- Checkpoint T-002-step2 loaded
- Files in checkpoint.files restored to their original state
- Execution continues from step 3 (stepIndex + 1)
- No steps 1 or 2 re-executed
- T-002 status transitions: `interrupted` → `in_progress` → `completed`
- Session Log updated with new session entry

---

## TC-RESUME-02: Stale Lock Detection

**Setup**: `memory.md` with task T-001 status=`in_progress`, owner=`S-OLD-123456` (not current session). No task with status=`interrupted`.

**Expected**:
- T-001 detected as stale lock (in_progress but owner ≠ current session)
- T-001 treated as `interrupted`
- User presented with resume/rollback/skip options for T-001
- No work done until user selects an option
- FR-011 satisfied: stale in_progress treated as interrupted

---

## TC-RESUME-03: Malformed memory.md — FR-016 Repair Flow

**Setup**: `memory.md` has task T-001 in Task Registry but no corresponding Task Details subsection (Validation Rule 1 violation).

**Expected**:
- Plugin halts immediately
- Output includes: "memory.md validation failed: Rule 1 — T-001 in Task Registry has no corresponding Task Details subsection."
- Offers: (a) reset to last valid checkpoint, (b) archive and start fresh
- No tasks executed until user selects option

---

## TC-RESUME-04: No Interrupted Tasks (All Pending)

**Setup**: `memory.md` with 3 tasks all status=`pending`, no `in_progress` or `interrupted`.

**Expected**:
- No interrupted task warnings
- Session menu shows pending tasks and asks user to confirm execution scope
- Proceeds to execute selected tasks after confirmation

---

## TC-RESUME-05: Missing Checkpoint File

**Setup**: T-002 status=`interrupted`, Checkpoint Registry points to `T-002-step1-S-001` but the JSON file is absent from `.kandalf/checkpoints/`.

**Expected**:
- Warning: "Warning: checkpoint file for T-002 not found. Rollback is still available via .kandalf/rollbacks/T-002/. Choose rollback or skip."
- Only rollback or skip offered (resume not available without checkpoint file)
- No crash

---

## TC-RESUME-06: No memory.md

**Setup**: No `memory.md` at project root.

**Expected**:
- Exact output: `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf start to begin a new session.`

📂 FILE: ./tests/integration/test-rollback.md

# Integration Test: /kandalf rollback

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-01: Per-Task Rollback Isolates Correct Files

**Setup**: Two completed tasks: T-001 modified `prompts/system.md`, T-002 modified `tools/search.json`. Both have rollback copies.

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf rollback T-001`. Confirm preview.

**Expected**:
- `prompts/system.md` restored to pre-T-001 content
- `tools/search.json` unchanged (still T-002's version)
- T-001 status: `pending` in memory.md
- T-001 Owner, Checkpoint: cleared
- Change Log: new `rolled-back` entries for T-001 files
- CodeGraph index synced after rollback
- T-002 status: `completed` (unchanged)

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-02: --all Reverses All in Correct Order

**Setup**: Three completed tasks: T-001, T-002, T-003 each modifying different files. Rollback copies exist for all.

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf rollback --all`. Confirm.

**Expected**:
- T-003 files restored first, then T-002, then T-001 (reverse registration order)
- All three tasks set to `pending`
- All files restored to pre-kandalf originals
- CodeGraph synced once at end

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-03: --to S-XXX Restores to Session Boundary

**Setup**: T-001 completed in session S-001. T-002 completed in session S-002. Rolling back `--to S-001`.

**Expected**:
- T-002 rolled back (completed after S-001 ended)
- T-001 NOT rolled back (completed during S-001)
- Project state matches what it was at S-001's end time

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-04: Non-Completed Task Error

**Setup**: T-003 status=`pending`.

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf rollback T-003`.

**Expected**:
- Output: `Error: T-003 has status 'pending' — only completed tasks can be rolled back. Use /kandalf status to inspect.`
- No file changes

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-05: Missing Rollback Files

**Setup**: T-001 completed but `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-001/` is absent.

**Expected**:
- Output: `Error: rollback files for T-001 not found in .kandalf/rollbacks/. Manual recovery required — see memory.md Change Log for the original content.`
- No partial restore

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-06: User Cancels at Preview

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf rollback T-001`. At preview, choose cancel/no.

**Expected**: No files modified. No memory.md changes.

---

## TC-ROLLBACK-07: CodeGraph Synced After Rollback

**Steps**: Complete TC-ROLLBACK-01.

**Expected**: `codegraph sync` was run after restore. `codegraph status` shows fresh index reflecting rolled-back files.

# Integration Test: Dynamic Compression Across One Session

Validates the end-to-end category-switching behavior of `lib/compression-policy.md`
(specs/016-context7-caveman-integration, User Story 1).

**Setup**: caveman skill installed; session started with defaults (`compression_enabled: true`); a registered plan containing at least one small `analysis` task and one `change` task.

---

## Step 1 — Simple: /kandalf:status

**Expected**: Reply is caveman `full` — terse fragments around an intact Task Registry table; status icons, IDs, and the exact output format block unchanged.

## Step 2 — Complex: execute a change task

**Expected**:
- Sub-agent spawned with premium tier (`opus`) and `lite` compression directive.
- Conversational progress/result replies are caveman `lite`: no filler, full technical nuance, code/paths byte-preserved.
- memory.md writes (Task Details, Change Log) are normal full prose.

## Step 3 — Planning: /kandalf:report composition

**Expected**:
- The report FILE (stdout block or `--output` target) is normal full prose, fully uncompressed (CT-COMP-ARTIFACT-01).
- The conversational wrap-up around it follows the policy (compressed).

## Step 4 — Cross-check

**Expected**: Same session, three different styles observed (full → lite → uncompressed artifact), with `compression_enabled: true` unchanged in memory.md Config throughout.

📂 FILE: ./tests/integration/test-update-docs.md

# Integration Test: /kandalf update-docs

## Test Environment

- Project: `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/` (1 README + 1 system-prompt + 1 conversation example)
- `memory.md` must exist at project root (created by a prior `/kandalf:start` run or manually seeded for test)
- `.codegraph/` must exist (run `codegraph init -i` first)
- `docs_path` must be set in memory.md Config before each test case

---

## User Story 1: Sync Docs After Code Changes

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-01: Happy Path — Standard Doc Update

**Setup**:
- Valid `memory.md` with `docs_path: tests/fixtures/sample-docs/` and `last_docs_update: null`
- `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/README.md` contains a section with a symbol reference (e.g., backtick-quoted function name) that matches a symbol known to CodeGraph
- That symbol's description has changed in the codebase since the doc was written (simulate by modifying the relevant lib file)

**Steps**:
1. Run `/kandalf update-docs`
2. Confirm planned changes when prompted (accept all)

**Expected**:
- Output: `Documentation update complete.` with `Sections updated: ≥ 1`
- The referenced section in `README.md` contains updated content reflecting current code state
- `last_docs_update` in memory.md Config is set to an ISO 8601 timestamp matching the current run
- `last_docs_update_session` is set to the current session ID
- memory.md Task Registry has ≥ 1 new completed task for this run
- `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` contains a copy of the original README.md (rollback available)
- `.kandalf/checkpoints/{checkpointId}.json` exists for the update task

**Fail if**:
- No sections updated despite symbol-change setup
- `last_docs_update` not set after successful completion
- No rollback copy or checkpoint created
- Files outside `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/` are modified

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-02: Error — No memory.md

**Setup**: No `memory.md` at project root.

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs`

**Expected**:
- Error: `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to initialize.`
- No files scanned; no docs modified

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-03: Error — Missing Docs Path

**Setup**: Valid `memory.md` without `docs_path` Config key. No `--docs` flag.

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs`

**Expected**:
- Error: `Error: docs path not found. Provide --docs <path> or run /kandalf:start first.`
- No files modified

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-04: --docs Flag Overrides memory.md docs_path

**Setup**: `memory.md` has `docs_path: tests/fixtures/sample-docs/`. Valid alternate docs directory at `tests/fixtures/alt-docs/` (can be an empty dir with one stub .md file).

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs --docs tests/fixtures/alt-docs/`

**Expected**:
- Command scans `tests/fixtures/alt-docs/` (not `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/`)
- `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/` files are NOT touched

---

## User Story 2: Enrich Sparse Documentation

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-05: Enrichment Mode — Stub Section Enriched

**Setup**:
- Valid `memory.md`
- Test doc file `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/README.md` has two sections:
  - Section A: `## Overview` — body has 2 lines (stub threshold: ≤ 3)
  - Section B: `## Usage` — body has 10+ lines (well-documented, above threshold)

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs --enrich`

**Confirm changes when prompted.**

**Expected**:
- Section A (`## Overview`) body is now longer than 2 lines
- Section A body is wrapped: starts with `<!-- kpw:enriched -->`, ends with `<!-- /kpw:enriched -->`
- Section B (`## Usage`) body is UNCHANGED (not a stub; enrichment does not touch it)
- Output: `Sections enriched: 1`
- Rollback copy of the modified doc file exists in `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/`

**Fail if**:
- Section B is modified
- Section A enriched content lacks `<!-- kpw:enriched -->` / `<!-- /kpw:enriched -->` wrapper
- `--enrich` absent version of this test enriches Section A (enrichment must be opt-in)

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-06: Enrichment Off by Default — Stubs Preserved

**Setup**: Same as TC-UPDATEDOCS-05 (stub section present).

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs` (NO `--enrich` flag)

**Expected**:
- Section A (`## Overview`) body is UNCHANGED
- No `<!-- kpw:enriched -->` markers appear
- Output: `Sections enriched: 0`

---

## User Story 3: Session-Aware Delta Updates

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-07: Second Run — No Changes → Up To Date

**Setup**:
- TC-UPDATEDOCS-01 has already been run; `last_docs_update` is set in memory.md
- No code changes have been made since TC-UPDATEDOCS-01

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs` (second run)

**Expected**:
- Output: `Documentation is already up to date.`
- Zero files modified
- `last_docs_update` timestamp in memory.md is the SAME value as after TC-UPDATEDOCS-01 (not advanced)
- No new tasks added to Task Registry

**Fail if**:
- Any doc file is modified
- `last_docs_update` is advanced despite no changes

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-08: --dry-run — Plans Without Writing

**Setup**: Project with pending documentation changes (simulate: ensure `last_docs_update` is null OR a code file was changed after last update).

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs --dry-run`

**Expected**:
- Output includes planned-changes table with ≥ 1 row (at least one candidate identified)
- Output ends with: `Dry run — no files modified.`
- Zero doc files modified
- `memory.md` Task Registry unchanged (no new task rows added)
- `last_docs_update` NOT set or advanced

---

### TC-UPDATEDOCS-09: Budget Interruption Mid-Run

**Setup**:
- `budget_threshold_pct: 10` (low threshold)
- Multiple doc files requiring updates
- Valid `.codegraph/` index

**Input**: `/kandalf update-docs`

**Expected** (when threshold is crossed mid-run):
- Current atomic step completes (no partial file write)
- Checkpoint written for the in-progress doc update task
- In-progress task status set to `interrupted` in memory.md
- Session Handoff Summary output and appended to memory.md
- `last_docs_update` NOT advanced (incomplete run)
- State is resumable: `/kandalf:resume` can continue the remaining doc tasks
- `memory.md` passes the validation checklist (`lib/common-preamble.md`, Rules 1–6) after shutdown

**Fail if**:
- `last_docs_update` is written despite incomplete run
- Any doc file left in a partially-written state
- memory.md fails validation rules after shutdown

# Integration Test: /kandalf start

## Test Environment
- Project: `tests/fixtures/sample-docs/` (1 README + 1 system-prompt + 1 conversation example)
- `.codegraph/` must exist (run `codegraph init -i` on fixtures dir first)
- No `memory.md` present at start of each test case

---

## TC-START-01: Happy Path — Valid Docs, Full Discovery

**Setup**: No `memory.md`. `--docs tests/fixtures/sample-docs/`.

**Steps**:
1. Run `/kandalf start --docs tests/fixtures/sample-docs/`
2. Answer discovery Q&A with goal: "Improve instruction clarity"
3. Accept proposed task plan

**Expected**:
- `memory.md` created at project root
- Config section populated: `budget_limit`, `budget_threshold_pct: 80`, `session_count: 1`, `created` date
- Project Goal and Scope sections non-empty
- Task Registry has ≥ 1 task row; all tasks have status `pending` or `completed`
- All registered tasks have corresponding Task Details subsections
- At least one Session Log entry for current session
- `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json` exists
- No files modified without a corresponding rollback copy in `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/`
- No files modified without a checkpoint in `.kandalf/checkpoints/`

**Fail if**:
- `memory.md` not created
- Task registered but no corresponding Task Details subsection (Validation Rule 1)
- Any file modified without rollback copy or checkpoint

---

## TC-START-02: Error — Missing Docs Path

**Setup**: No `memory.md`. `--docs /path/that/does/not/exist`.

**Expected**:
- Error output: `Error: documentation directory not found at '/path/that/does/not/exist'. Provide a valid --docs path.`
- No `memory.md` created
- No `.kandalf/` directory created

---

## TC-START-03: Error — Pre-existing Valid memory.md

**Setup**: Valid `memory.md` exists from a prior run.

**Expected**:
- Error: `Error: memory.md already exists. Run /kandalf resume to continue, or /kandalf status to inspect current state.`
- Existing `memory.md` unchanged

---

## TC-START-04: Error — Pre-existing Malformed memory.md

**Setup**: `memory.md` exists but is missing the Task Details section (Validation Rule 1 violation).

**Expected**:
- Plugin halts
- Diagnosis output: "memory.md validation failed: Rule 1 — task T-001 in Task Registry has no corresponding Task Details subsection."
- User offered: (a) reset to last checkpoint, (b) archive and start fresh
- No work done until user selects an option

---

## TC-START-05: Budget Interruption Mid-Task

**Setup**: `budget_threshold_pct: 10` in memory.md Config (low threshold to trigger quickly). Valid docs.

**Steps**:
1. Run `/kandalf start --docs tests/fixtures/sample-docs/ --budget-threshold 10`
2. Complete discovery Q&A
3. Allow plugin to begin executing first task

**Expected** (when threshold is crossed):
- Current atomic step completes (no partial file write)
- Checkpoint written for in-progress task
- Task status set to `interrupted` (not `in_progress`)
- Session Handoff Summary appears in output and is appended to `memory.md`
- `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json` written with `outcome: interrupted-budget`
- Any `.tmp` file cleaned up or absent (rename completed or write not started)
- `memory.md` is parseable and passes the validation checklist (`lib/common-preamble.md`, Rules 1–6) after shutdown

---

## TC-START-06: CodeGraph Non-Fatal Degradation

**Setup**: CodeGraph not installed (no `codegraph` binary on PATH).

**Expected**:
- Warning emitted: "Warning: CodeGraph index initialization failed. Continuing without impact analysis..."
- Session proceeds (plugin degrades gracefully)
- `CodeGraph Impact` fields in Task Details show `—` (not populated)
- All other functionality unaffected

📂 FILE: ./tests/integration/test-archive-roundtrip.md

# Integration Test: Archive Round-Trip

**Validates**: SC-002, FR-004, FR-005, FR-006
**Fixture**: `tests/fixtures/archive-project/memory.md` (1131 lines pre-archive)

## Setup

1. Copy the fixture into a scratch project as `memory.md`; create `.kandalf/{archive,tmp,checkpoints,rollbacks}/`.
2. Confirm the fixture passes the validation checklist (`lib/common-preamble.md`).

## Scenario 1 — Full archive run with size assertion

1. Run a session to close (any command with a session-close step, e.g. resume with no pending work) so the archive trigger fires.
2. **Expect**: 9 archive records under `.kandalf/archive/` (default `archive_keep_sessions` = 1); memory.md retains session 10 in full.
3. **Size assertion (SC-002)**: post-archive memory.md line count ≤ 25% of 1131 (≤ 283 lines).
4. memory.md still passes the validation checklist (amended Rules 1 and 4 treat archived IDs as satisfied).

## Scenario 2 — Report retrieval of archived tasks

1. Run `/kandalf:report`.
2. **Expect**: report includes all 50 change tasks; tasks T-001…T-045 marked `(archived)`; data sourced from archive records without errors.

## Scenario 3 — Rollback retrieval of an archived task

1. Run `/kandalf:rollback T-010` (archived with S-20260502).
2. **Expect**: Task Details for T-010 restored into memory.md; status reset to `pending`; T-010 removed from the archive record's Task Details; Archive Index `Tasks Archived` cell updated; rollback proceeds against `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-010/` per the existing rollback flow.

## Scenario 4 — Interruption safety (kill mid-archive)

Interrupt the archive run at each protocol step and verify:

| Killed at | Expected state |
|---|---|
| Step 1 (writing `.archive.tmp`) | memory.md untouched and valid; stray `.tmp` cleaned on next session start |
| Step 2 (verify) | same as step 1 |
| Step 3 (rename to archive/) | archive file exists; memory.md untouched; re-run is idempotent (Scenario 4 of contract test) |
| Step 4 (memory.md tmp+rename) | if before rename: memory.md untouched; if after: archive complete and consistent |

**Pass criterion (FR-006)**: in every case memory.md passes validation and no history is lost (every task/session present either in memory.md or in an archive record).

## Results

| Scenario | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 — full archive + size | PASS (2026-06-10, mechanical simulation) | Protocol rules applied to the fixture by script: 1131 → 244 lines = 21% ≤ 25% (SC-002) |
| 2 — report retrieval | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | report SKILL.md Section 1 routes archived tasks through lib/archive-protocol.md retrieval table |
| 3 — rollback retrieval | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | rollback SKILL.md Section 1 step 4 implements restore + index update + missing-file halt |
| 4 — interruption safety | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | 4-step tmp/verify/rename protocol: memory.md untouched before step 4 rename; idempotent re-run rule covers step-3 kill |

*Scenario 1 verified by mechanically simulating the trimming rules; scenarios 2–4 by instruction-file conformance review. A live conversational run is recommended before release.*

# Integration Test: /kandalf report

---

## TC-REPORT-01: Report with 2+ Completed Change Tasks

**Setup**: `memory.md` with T-001 and T-002 both `completed` type=`change`. Rollback copies and CodeGraph Impact fields populated for both.

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf report`.

**Expected output contains**:
- `# kandalf Improvement Report` heading
- `## Baseline Assessment` section with symbol count
- `## Changes Applied` section with subsections for T-001 and T-002
- Each subsection has: What changed, Rationale, Impact radius, Before snippet, After snippet, Test example
- `## Delta Summary` table
- `## Recommended Next Steps` section (if pending tasks exist)

---

## TC-REPORT-02: Missing Rollback Copy (Non-Fatal Warning)

**Setup**: T-001 completed, but `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-001/` deleted.

**Expected**:
- Warning: `Warning: original content for T-001 not available — before/after comparison omitted for this task.`
- Report still generated for other tasks
- T-001 section present but before/after snippet replaced with warning

---

## TC-REPORT-03: No Completed Tasks — Error

**Setup**: All tasks `pending` or `deferred`.

**Expected**:
- Exact output: `No completed changes found. Run /kandalf status to see pending tasks, or /kandalf resume to continue working.`

---

## TC-REPORT-04: --output Flag Writes to File

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf report --output ./improvement-report.md`.

**Expected**:
- File `improvement-report.md` created at project root
- File contains full report (same as stdout)
- Output to console: path to generated file

---

## TC-REPORT-05: --format text Output

**Steps**: Run `/kandalf report --format text`.

**Expected**:
- Output is plain text (no Markdown heading syntax)
- All sections still present, formatted as plain text

# Integration Test: Knowledge Registry Lifecycle (Two-Session Bridge)

Validates User Story 1 of `specs/015-knowledge-registry/spec.md` end to end: knowledge captured in one session bridges into the next. Exercises OP-1, OP-2, OP-3, OP-4, OP-5, OP-6, OP-8 of `lib/knowledge-protocol.md`.

---

## IT-KNOW-01: First Session Initializes and Captures

**Setup**: A sample agent project with docs but no `knowledge/` directory and no `memory.md`.

**Steps**:

1. Run `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs` with goal "improve intent classification accuracy".
2. Verify output contains `Knowledge registry initialized at 'knowledge/'.` and discovery proceeds with no prior-knowledge citations (acceptance scenario US1-3).
3. Complete one `change` task successfully (T-001).
4. Attempt a second task (T-002) and roll it back via `/kandalf:rollback T-002`.
5. Mid-session, tell the harness: "remember that the eval set is the source of truth for accuracy claims".
6. End the session gracefully.

**Expected**:

- `knowledge/entries/` contains three notes: a `success` for T-001, a `failure` for T-002 (with `## Retry Conditions`), and an `insight` from step 5 (confirmed with `Recorded [[K-003…]] (insight): …`).
- `knowledge/index.md` lists all three in their type sections (dual-update).
- `knowledge/timeline.md` has one session section with the goal, outcome, all three wikilinks, and a `Reversal:` line for T-002.
- All three entry files existed on disk BEFORE session end (incremental persistence — verify mtimes precede shutdown).

---

## IT-KNOW-02: Second Session Bridges the Knowledge

**Setup**: The project state produced by IT-KNOW-01.

**Steps**:

1. Run `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs` with a goal that overlaps T-002's failed approach.
2. Observe startup output.
3. Proceed through discovery and task decomposition.

**Expected**:

- Startup prints the load summary (`Knowledge registry: … of 3 total.`); no re-initialization message.
- During discovery/planning, at least one prior learning is cited without the user re-explaining history (SC-001).
- When decomposition proposes the approach recorded in T-002's failure entry, the harness presents `Prior failure on record: [[…]] … Proceed anyway, adapt, or skip?` and does NOT adopt it silently (SC-002).
- Choosing "adapt" records the adapted approach as a task; the failure entry remains untouched (`status: active`, no deletion).

---

## IT-KNOW-03: Resume Path Loads Knowledge

**Setup**: The IT-KNOW-01 project with an interrupted session (kill during a task; checkpoint exists).

**Steps**:

1. Run `/kandalf:resume`.

**Expected**:

- After memory.md validation, the load summary line appears (OP-2 runs on resume too).
- The interrupted-task resolution menu cites a relevant registry entry when it changes the resume-vs-rollback recommendation (e.g., a recorded failure for a similar approach favors rollback, citing the entry).

---

## IT-KNOW-04: Flat-Cost Startup at Scale (SC-006)

**Setup**: Copy `tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/` into a sample project as `knowledge/`, then generate 200 additional minimal `insight` entries (valid frontmatter, unique K-IDs, tags disjoint from the session goal) and append matching index rows.

**Steps**:

1. Run `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs` with goal "improve guardrails".

**Expected**:

- Startup reads only: `index.md`, the 3 most recent timeline sections, active failures/constraints, and goal-tag-matched entries — NOT the 200 off-topic insights (verify via the load summary: `{N}` stays small while `{T}` ≈ 208).
- Discovery preparation completes in roughly the same time as with the 8-entry fixture (no full-vault ingestion).

---

## IT-KNOW-05: Tolerance Does Not Break the Bridge

**Setup**: The fixture vault (includes malformed `K-009-malformed-entry.md`).

**Steps**:

1. Run `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs`.

**Expected**:

- Exactly one `Warning: knowledge entry 'entries/K-009-malformed-entry.md' could not be parsed …` line; session continues; K-009 excluded from counts and citations; all other entries load normally.

📂 FILE: ./tests/integration/test-preview-mode.md

# Integration Test: Plan Preview Mode (start --dry-run)

**Validates**: FR-012, FR-013, SC-005 (composes with feature 013's start/execute split)

## Scenario 1 — Preview produces a plan and executes nothing

1. In a fresh project (no `memory.md`), run:
   `/kandalf:start --dry-run --docs ./docs`
2. **Expect**:
   - Discovery Q&A and task decomposition complete; integrity Rules 7–9 pass.
   - The full task plan (Task IDs, titles, types, dependencies, efforts) and a
     total budget estimate are printed.
   - `memory.md` is created with all tasks registered as `pending`.
   - **No task is executed.** No file outside `memory.md` and `.kandalf/`
     is created or modified (verify with a file-tree diff).
   - Session Log row closed with outcome `completed`.
   - Output ends with the preview-reuse hint (exact text):
     ```
     Plan preview complete — no tasks were executed.
     The plan is saved in memory.md. Run /kandalf:execute to run it,
     or /kandalf:status to review it. Discovery will not be repeated.
     ```
   - The "run execute now?" prompt is NOT shown.

## Scenario 2 — Plan reuse without repeating discovery

1. After Scenario 1, run `/kandalf:execute` (feature 013) — or, until 013
   ships, `/kandalf:resume`.
2. **Expect**: the registered pending tasks are picked up directly; no discovery
   Q&A questions are asked again; execution proceeds in dependency order.

## Scenario 3 — Dry-run on an existing project

1. With a valid `memory.md` present, run `/kandalf:start --dry-run --docs ./docs`.
2. **Expect**: the existing-memory error from start Section 2a (unchanged behavior —
   `--dry-run` does not bypass the pre-check).

## Results

| Scenario | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 — preview, no execution | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | start SKILL.md Preview-exit block: stops before Section 8, prints exact reuse hint, closes session `completed`, no-side-effect guarantee stated |
| 2 — reuse without discovery | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | Plan persists as `pending` tasks in memory.md; execute/resume pick up the registry without re-running discovery |
| 3 — existing-project pre-check | PASS (2026-06-10, desk-check) | Preview exit sits after Section 2a; `--dry-run` does not bypass the existing-memory error |

*Desk-check = instruction-file conformance review. A live preview session is recommended before release.*

# Sample Agent: Customer Support Bot

This is a minimal test fixture for kandalf integration tests.

## Purpose

This agent handles tier-1 customer support inquiries for a SaaS product.
It answers questions about billing, account access, and feature usage.

## Scope

- Billing questions (invoice, payment method, plan changes)
- Account access (password reset, 2FA, locked accounts)
- Feature usage (how-to questions for core product features)

## Out of Scope

- Technical bugs (escalate to engineering)
- Sales inquiries (forward to sales team)
- Legal or compliance questions (forward to legal)

A populated `memory.md` representing a project with 10 terminal sessions and 50 tasks
(Change Log, Checkpoint Registry, and Task Details fully populated).

Used by:
- `tests/contract/test-archive.md` — archive trigger eligibility, record format, Archive Index rows

  report/rollback retrieval of archived tasks, mid-archive interruption safety

The fixture state is intentionally oversized so that the 3-most-recent-terminal-sessions
retention rule archives 7 sessions on the first run.

📂 FILE: ./tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/entries/K-001-prompt-sectioning-decision.md
==============================
---
id: K-001
title: Adopt sectioned system prompts over monolithic blocks
type: decision
status: active
created: 2026-06-01T09:42:00
session: S-20260601-090000
task: T-001
tags: [knowledge-registry, prompts, architecture]
---

## Description

System prompts in this agent are split into named sections (role, constraints, examples, output format) instead of one monolithic block.

## Rationale

Sectioned prompts made targeted edits reviewable and let CodeGraph impact analysis attribute regressions to a specific section. The monolithic variant caused unrelated behavior shifts on every edit.

## Context

Related: [[K-004-token-budget-constraint]]. Files touched: `prompts/system.md`.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/entries/K-003-guardrail-extraction-success.md
==============================
---
id: K-003
title: Extracting guardrails into a dedicated section cut jailbreak rate
type: success
status: active
created: 2026-06-03T14:35:00
session: S-20260603-140000
task: T-005
tags: [knowledge-registry, guardrails, safety]
---

## Description

Moved scattered guardrail instructions into a single dedicated constraints section near the top of the system prompt.

## Rationale

Red-team suite jailbreak rate fell from 7% to 2% with no measurable loss on helpfulness metrics. Co-locating the rules removed contradictions that earlier edits had introduced.

## Context

Related: [[K-001-prompt-sectioning-decision]]. Blast radius (CodeGraph): 3 files, 14 symbols.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/entries/K-007-central-tone-guide.md
==============================
---
id: K-007
title: Centralize tone rules in one shared guide
type: decision
status: active
created: 2026-06-05T11:40:00
session: S-20260605-100000
task: T-009
tags: [knowledge-registry, tone, templates]
supersedes: "[[K-006-inline-tone-rules]]"
---

## Description

Tone rules live in a single shared guide referenced by all intent templates.

## Rationale

Inline duplication (see [[K-006-inline-tone-rules]]) drifted: three templates contradicted each other on formality within two sessions. Centralizing removed the drift class entirely.

## Context

Supersedes [[K-006-inline-tone-rules]].

==============================
📂 FILE: ./tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/entries/K-004-token-budget-constraint.md
==============================
---
id: K-004
title: System prompt must stay under 3000 tokens
type: constraint
status: active
created: 2026-06-03T16:02:00
session: S-20260603-140000
tags: [knowledge-registry, token-budget, performance]
---

## Description

The deployed model truncates context beyond budget; the system prompt has a hard ceiling of 3000 tokens before retrieval results stop fitting.

## Rationale

Two incidents where enriched prompts silently evicted retrieval context. Any prompt-growing change must be paired with a token count check.

## Context

Related: [[K-003-guardrail-extraction-success]].

==============================
📂 FILE: ./tests/fixtures/knowledge-sample/entries/K-002-fewshot-reordering-failure.md
==============================
---
id: K-002
title: Reordering few-shot examples by recency degraded intent accuracy
type: failure
status: active
created: 2026-06-01T15:10:00
session: S-20260601-090000
task: T-003
tags: [knowledge-registry, few-shot, intent-classification]
---

## Description

Attempted to reorder few-shot examples most-recent-first to bias the model toward new product flows.

## Rationale

Intent accuracy on the regression set dropped from 94% to 81%. The model anchored on the first two examples regardless of recency, so reordering displaced the canonical examples that carried most of the signal.

## Context

Related: [[K-001-prompt-sectioning-decision]].

## Retry Conditions

Retry only with a regression set that includes the new product flows, and keep the two canonical anchor examples in positions 1–2.

📂 FILE: ./docs/05-architecture.md

---
title: "Architecture Guide"
tags: [kandalf, architecture, developer, diagrams, mermaid]

slug: architecture
---

# Architecture Guide

> Developer reference: session lifecycle, component architecture, task execution protocol, and sub-agent coordination model.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[concepts/05-sub-agent-model|Sub-Agent Model]] · [[concepts/04-budget-detection|Budget Detection]]

---

## Overview

kandalf is a Claude Code plugin that systematically improves conversational agent projects. It coordinates the following components:

- **Plugin Skill Layer** — the six SKILL.md command files loaded by Claude Code
- **Shared Preamble** — `lib/common-preamble.md`, the single authoritative statement of the validation checklist, ID formats, and the dual-update convention; every command reads it first and none restates it
- **memory.md** — the single shared state file; all coordination happens through it
- **Sub-Agent Pool** — parallel or sequential Claude agents spawned for task execution
- **Checkpoint System** — 6-step atomic write protocol ensuring no data loss
- **Rollback Store** — pre-task file copies in `.kandalf/rollbacks/`
- **CodeGraph MCP** — semantic impact analysis before every file modification
- **Budget Tracker** — running token estimate that triggers graceful shutdown

The diagrams below describe how these components interact.

### Reference Loading (scoped, per command)

Commands no longer load all `lib/` documents unconditionally. Each SKILL.md declares an invoke-time `**References**` list plus lazy references loaded only at their point of need:

| Command | Invoke-time (besides `common-preamble.md`) | Lazy |
|---|---|---|
| start | memory-schema, codegraph-integration, budget-detection | checkpoint-protocol (task execution), archive-protocol (session close), templates |
| resume | memory-schema, checkpoint-protocol, budget-detection | archive-protocol (session close) |
| status | — | memory-schema (repair flow), archive-protocol (`--verbose` archived tasks) |
| rollback | checkpoint-protocol | memory-schema (repair flow), archive-protocol (archived-task retrieval) |
| report | codegraph-integration | memory-schema (repair flow), archive-protocol (archived-task retrieval) |
| update-docs | codegraph-integration | memory-schema (registration), checkpoint-protocol + budget-detection (execution), `skills/update-docs/reference.md` (analysis) |

If a command file ever conflicts with `lib/common-preamble.md`, the preamble wins.

---

## Diagram 1 — Session Lifecycle

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A([claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf]) --> B["/kandalf:start --docs path"]
    B --> C[Input Ingestion<br/>docs / project / examples / requirements]
    C --> D[CodeGraph Check<br/>codegraph init -i<br/>baseline metrics captured]
    D --> E[Discovery Q&A<br/>goals · scope · constraints · success criteria]
    E --> F[Task Decomposition<br/>atomic tasks registered in memory.md<br/>codegraph_impact on every change task]
    F --> G{User approves<br/>task plan?}
    G -- No --> E
    G -- Yes --> H[Task Execution Loop]

    H --> I{Next task<br/>available?}
    I -- Yes --> J[Claim task<br/>status → in_progress<br/>owner ← sessionId]
    J --> K{Parallelizable?}
    K -- Yes --> L[Spawn sub-agents<br/>in parallel]
    K -- No --> M[Execute inline<br/>or single sub-agent]
    L --> N[Checkpoint protocol<br/>6 steps per file write]
    M --> N
    N --> O{Budget<br/>threshold<br/>crossed?}
    O -- Yes --> P[Graceful Shutdown<br/>checkpoint saved<br/>status → interrupted<br/>Session Handoff Summary]
    O -- No --> Q{Task<br/>verified?}
    Q -- Pass --> R[status → completed]
    Q -- Fail --> J
    R --> I
    I -- No more tasks --> S["/kandalf:report"]

    P --> T["/kandalf:resume"]
    T --> U[Resolve interrupted tasks<br/>resume / rollback / defer]
    U --> H
```

**Session Lifecycle**: Shows the complete state machine for a kandalf session. Starting with `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf`, the session flows through Input Ingestion → CodeGraph Check → Discovery Q&A → Task Decomposition → User Approval → Task Execution Loop. Within the loop: tasks are claimed (`in_progress`), executed (inline or via parallel sub-agents), and checkpoint-protected on every file write. If the budget threshold is crossed, the session gracefully shuts down and waits for `/kandalf:resume`. Once all tasks complete, the session advances to `/kandalf:report`.

---

## Diagram 2 — Component Architecture

```mermaid
graph LR
    subgraph PluginLayer["Plugin Skill Layer (kandalf/skills/)"]
        S1[start.md]
        S2[resume.md]
        S3[status.md]
        S4[rollback.md]
        S5[report.md]
        S6[update-docs.md]
    end

    subgraph State["Session State"]
        M[(memory.md<br/>Task Registry<br/>Change Log<br/>Session Log<br/>Checkpoint Registry)]
    end

    subgraph Safety["Write Safety"]
        CP[Checkpoint System<br/>.kandalf/checkpoints/]
        RB[Rollback Store<br/>.kandalf/rollbacks/]
        TMP[Temp Staging<br/>.kandalf/tmp/]
    end

    subgraph Agents["Execution Layer"]
        PA[Parent Session]
        SA1[Sub-Agent 1]
        SA2[Sub-Agent 2]
        SA3[Sub-Agent N]
    end

    CG[(CodeGraph MCP<br/>codegraph_impact<br/>codegraph_search<br/>codegraph_context)]
    BT[Budget Tracker<br/>running token estimate]
    YAP[(Your Agent Project<br/>target files)]

    PluginLayer --> PA
    PA -- reads/writes --> M
    PA -- spawns --> SA1
    PA -- spawns --> SA2
    PA -- spawns --> SA3
    PA -- monitors --> BT

    SA1 -- queries --> CG
    SA2 -- queries --> CG
    SA1 -- writes rollback --> RB
    SA1 -- writes checkpoint --> CP
    SA1 -- writes tmp --> TMP
    TMP -- atomic rename --> YAP
    SA1 -- updates --> M
    SA2 -- updates --> M
```

**Component Architecture**: Shows how the six plugin skills (`start`, `resume`, `status`, `rollback`, `report`, `update-docs`) feed into a Parent Session, which reads and writes the shared `memory.md` state file. The Parent Session spawns parallel sub-agents that query the CodeGraph MCP for impact analysis, write rollback copies and checkpoint JSON to the Safety layer, and stage writes via the Temp Staging area before atomically renaming them to the target files in Your Agent Project. A Budget Tracker monitors token usage throughout.

---

## Diagram 3 — Task Execution & Checkpoint Protocol

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant PS as Parent Session
    participant SA as Sub-Agent
    participant CG as CodeGraph MCP
    participant MEM as memory.md
    participant FS as FileSystem

    PS->>MEM: Claim task — status → in_progress, owner ← sessionId
    PS->>SA: Spawn sub-agent with task instructions + lib references

    SA->>CG: codegraph_search(target symbol)
    CG-->>SA: Symbol location confirmed

    SA->>CG: codegraph_impact(symbol, depth=3)
    CG-->>SA: Blast radius: N symbols across M files

    alt Impact > 20 symbols
        SA->>PS: HALT — impact too large, request split or confirmation
        PS->>SA: Confirmed / task split
    end

    SA->>MEM: Log impact to CodeGraph Impact field (Task Details)

    Note over SA,FS: Checkpoint Protocol begins (6 steps)

    SA->>FS: Step 1 — copy original → .kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/
    SA->>MEM: Step 2 — append Change Log entry (status: planned)
    SA->>FS: Step 3 — write checkpoint JSON → .kandalf/checkpoints/
    SA->>MEM: Step 3 — add row to Checkpoint Registry
    SA->>FS: Step 4 — write new content → .kandalf/tmp/{file}.tmp
    SA->>FS: Step 5 — atomic rename tmp → target path
    SA->>MEM: Step 6 — update Change Log entry (status: applied)

    SA->>CG: codegraph_affected([modified files])
    CG-->>SA: Test files to re-run

    SA->>MEM: Update task: status → completed, Owner cleared
    SA->>MEM: Write to Session Log

    PS->>PS: Budget check — proceed to next task or graceful shutdown
```

**Task Execution & Checkpoint Protocol**: A sequence diagram showing the interaction between Parent Session, Sub-Agent, CodeGraph MCP, memory.md, and the filesystem during a single `change` task. Key steps: (1) Parent claims the task in memory.md. (2) Sub-Agent calls `codegraph_search` to locate the target symbol, then `codegraph_impact` to get the blast radius. (3) If impact > 20 symbols, the sub-agent halts and waits for confirmation. (4) The 6-step checkpoint protocol begins: rollback copy → Change Log planned → checkpoint JSON → tmp write → atomic rename → Change Log applied. (5) `codegraph_affected` identifies test files. (6) Task marked `completed` in memory.md. (7) Parent runs budget check.

---

## Diagram 4 — Sub-Agent Coordination Model

```mermaid
graph TD
    PS[Parent Session] --> CHECK{Check Task<br/>Registry in memory.md}

    CHECK --> PAR{Tasks<br/>parallelizable?}

    PAR -- Yes, no overlapping Outputs --> SPAWN[Spawn sub-agents concurrently]
    PAR -- No, shared Outputs --> SEQ[Execute sequentially<br/>one sub-agent at a time]
    PAR -- Small analysis task --> INLINE[Execute inline<br/>no sub-agent spawn]

    SPAWN --> SA1[Sub-Agent A<br/>Task T-003<br/>prompts/system.md]
    SPAWN --> SA2[Sub-Agent B<br/>Task T-004<br/>config/tools.json]

    SA1 --> OC1{File ownership<br/>check before<br/>each write}
    SA2 --> OC2{File ownership<br/>check before<br/>each write}

    OC1 -- File owned by another in-progress task --> HALT1[HALT<br/>alert parent session]
    OC1 -- File available --> WRITE1[Execute checkpoint<br/>protocol → write file]

    OC2 -- File owned by another in-progress task --> HALT2[HALT<br/>alert parent session]
    OC2 -- File available --> WRITE2[Execute checkpoint<br/>protocol → write file]

    WRITE1 --> DONE1[Update memory.md<br/>status → completed]
    WRITE2 --> DONE2[Update memory.md<br/>status → completed]

    DONE1 --> AGG[Parent Session<br/>aggregates results]
    DONE2 --> AGG

    SEQ --> SA3[Sub-Agent C<br/>sequential execution]
    SA3 --> DONE3[Update memory.md<br/>status → completed]
    DONE3 --> AGG

    INLINE --> AGG

    AGG --> NEXT[Advance to next<br/>task in dependency order]
```

**Sub-Agent Coordination Model**: Shows how the Parent Session decides whether to spawn parallel sub-agents, a single sequential sub-agent, or execute inline. Parallel tasks (no overlapping output files) spawn concurrent sub-agents. Each sub-agent performs a file ownership check before any write — if another in-progress task owns the file, it halts and alerts the parent. When the write is safe, the sub-agent executes the checkpoint protocol and updates memory.md. The parent aggregates all sub-agent results and advances to the next task in dependency order.

---

## Key Design Invariants

These invariants are enforced throughout the codebase. Contributions must not violate them.

| Invariant | Rule |
|-----------|------|
| State-first | No file in the agent project is touched until the task is registered in `memory.md` |
| Rollback-first | No file is modified without a rollback copy written first (Checkpoint Step 1) |
| Atomic writes | Files are never written directly — always via tmp → rename (Checkpoint Steps 4–5) |
| Impact-first | `codegraph_impact` is always called before the first file write of any `change` task |
| Ownership | A sub-agent never modifies a file owned by another in-progress task |
| No abandoned writes | Graceful shutdown always completes the current atomic step before terminating |

---

## State File Locations

```
your-agent-project/
├── memory.md                          Session state (created at /kandalf:start)
└── .kandalf/
    ├── checkpoints/                   Checkpoint JSON per step per task
    ├── rollbacks/{taskId}/            Pre-task original file copies
    ├── sessions/{sessionId}.json      Full session execution trace
    └── tmp/                           Atomic write staging area
```

See [[06-state-files|State Files Reference]] for full layout details.

---

## Contributing

When adding a new command skill:

1. Create `kandalf/skills/{name}/SKILL.md`
2. Follow the 6-step checkpoint protocol for every file write
3. Follow the 10-step CodeGraph data flow for every `change` task
4. Read/write `memory.md` as the coordination point — never maintain separate state
5. Update [[00-index|docs/00-index.md]] with the new command

See [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol]], [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration]], and [[concepts/05-sub-agent-model]] for the full protocols.

📂 FILE: ./docs/03-use-guide.md

---
title: "Use Guide"
tags: [kandalf, use-guide, commands, overview]

# Use Guide

> Overview of all kandalf commands and when to use each one.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[01-installation|Installation Guide]] · [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]]

---

## Command Summary

| Command | Purpose | When to Run |
|---------|---------|------------|
| [[commands/01-start\|/kandalf:start]] | Start a new improvement session | First time working on a project, or after completing all previous tasks |
| [[commands/02-resume\|/kandalf:resume]] | Resume an interrupted session | After budget exhaustion, Claude Code restart, or manual pause |
| [[commands/03-status\|/kandalf:status]] | View task registry and session summary | Any time you want to see what's in progress or remaining |
| [[commands/04-rollback\|/kandalf:rollback]] | Undo completed task changes | When a change was incorrect or needs to be retried |
| [[commands/05-report\|/kandalf:report]] | Generate the improvement report | When at least one `change` task is completed |
| [[commands/06-update-docs\|/kandalf:update-docs]] | Sync documentation with current codebase using CodeGraph | After an improvement session to keep docs accurate |
| [[commands/07-iterate\|/kandalf:iterate]] | Refine requirements and register new tasks on an existing session | After completing all tasks, or mid-session when requirements change |
| [[commands/08-execute\|/kandalf:execute]] | Execute the approved task plan | After `start` presents the plan (or after `iterate` refines it) |

---

## Typical Session Sequence

```
1. /kandalf:start --docs ./docs
      │  Discovery Q&A → task plan → approval → execution
      │
      ├── /kandalf:execute          ← REQUIRED: approve plan and begin execution
      │       │  (or /kandalf:iterate first to refine, then execute)
      │       │
      │       ├── (execution completes normally)
      │       │       ├── /kandalf:report
      │       │       ├── /kandalf:iterate           (optional — refine, add new tasks)
      │       │       │       └── /kandalf:execute   (run new tasks)
      │       │       └── /kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs   (optional post-session)
      │
      └── (session interrupted by budget or restart)
              └── /kandalf:resume
                      │  Resolve interrupted tasks → execute pending tasks
                      ├── /kandalf:report
                      ├── /kandalf:iterate           (optional)
                      └── /kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs   (optional)
```

At any point, use `/kandalf:status` to inspect the current state.

> See [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]] for a complete worked walkthrough with real sample inputs and outputs at every step.

---

## Loading the Plugin

Every session must begin by loading the plugin:

```bash
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
```

The plugin is not persistent across Claude Code sessions — run this command each time.

---

## Command Details

### /kandalf:start

Runs the full discovery-to-execution pipeline. Asks you about your improvement goals, decomposes work into tasks, runs CodeGraph impact analysis on every `change` task, and executes with checkpoint safety.

**Quick start**:
```
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs --dry-run   # preview the plan only — register it, execute nothing
```

With `--dry-run`, the plan is saved in `memory.md` and can be executed later with `/kandalf:execute` — discovery is not repeated.

→ Full reference: [[commands/01-start]]

---

### /kandalf:resume

Resumes an interrupted session. Detects stale locks (tasks whose owner session no longer matches the current session), presents interrupted tasks with three resolution options per task (resume from checkpoint / roll back / defer), then continues with pending tasks.

```
/kandalf:resume
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/02-resume]]

---

### /kandalf:status

Shows the task registry, current budget usage, and CodeGraph index health. Use this any time to understand where a session is.

```
/kandalf:status
/kandalf:status --verbose   # full task detail blocks
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/03-status]]

---

### /kandalf:rollback

Restores files to their pre-task state. Shows a preview before making any change. Three forms: per-task, all tasks, or all tasks from a session boundary.

```
/kandalf:rollback T-003          # undo one task
/kandalf:rollback --all          # undo everything
/kandalf:rollback --to S-XXX     # undo from a session boundary
```

After rollback, affected tasks reset to `pending` and can be re-executed with `/kandalf:resume`.

→ Full reference: [[commands/04-rollback]]

---

### /kandalf:report

Generates a before/after improvement report for all completed `change` tasks. Includes CodeGraph impact data, before/after file snippets, and concrete test examples.

```
/kandalf:report
/kandalf:report --output ./report.md
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/05-report]]

---

### /kandalf:update-docs

Syncs your project's documentation with the current codebase using CodeGraph structural analysis. Detects which doc sections reference symbols that changed since the last update, rewrites those sections, and optionally enriches sparse sections with CodeGraph-derived content.

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs --enrich    # also fill sparse sections
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs --dry-run   # preview without writing
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/06-update-docs]]

---

### /kandalf:iterate

Opens a structured requirement-refinement conversation on top of an existing session. Lets you add new requirements, remove or narrow existing ones, and adjust scope — without losing any completed work. Registers new tasks ready for `/kandalf:execute` to run.

```
/kandalf:iterate
/kandalf:iterate --scope "tool definitions"   # focus on one area only
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/07-iterate]]

---

### /kandalf:execute

Executes the task plan produced by `/kandalf:start`. Run this after reviewing the plan, optionally after using `/kandalf:iterate` to refine requirements. Provides explicit prompts at every decision point (interrupted tasks, locked tasks, completion).

```
/kandalf:execute
/kandalf:execute --budget-threshold 60   # tighter budget for a quick run
```

→ Full reference: [[commands/08-execute]]

---

## Common Scenarios

### "I want to start improving my agent"

```
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs    # discovery Q&A + edge case Q&A + plan
/kandalf:execute                # review output above, then run this
```

### "I want to run the approved plan"

```
/kandalf:execute
```

### "I want to refine requirements before executing"

```
/kandalf:iterate        # refine requirements
/kandalf:execute        # then execute the updated plan
```

### "My session was interrupted — how do I continue?"

```
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
/kandalf:resume
```

### "I want to see what's been done and what's left"

```
/kandalf:status
```

### "A change was wrong — I need to undo it"

```
/kandalf:rollback T-NNN   # replace NNN with the task number from /status
```

### "I'm done — generate the report"

```
/kandalf:report --output ./improvement-report.md
```

### "I want to refine requirements after completing tasks"

```
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
/kandalf:iterate
# → converse about changes → confirm → new tasks registered
/kandalf:resume   # execute the new tasks
```

### "I want to refine requirements in just one area"

```
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
/kandalf:iterate --scope "system prompt"
```

---

## Architecture

To understand how these commands work internally — session lifecycle, sub-agent model, checkpoint safety, and CodeGraph integration — see [[05-architecture|Architecture Guide]].

📂 FILE: ./docs/01-installation.md

---
title: "Installation"
tags: [kandalf, installation, setup, getting-started]
aliases: [install, setup guide, getting started]
slug: installation
---

# Installation Guide

> Complete setup from a fresh machine to your first kandalf session.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[03-use-guide|Use Guide]] · [[commands/01-start|/kandalf:start]]

---

## Prerequisites

Before installing, confirm you have:

| Requirement | Check | Notes |
|-------------|-------|-------|
| **Claude Code** | `claude --version` | CLI, desktop app, or IDE extension |
| **Git** | `git --version` | Any recent version |
| **Node.js 20+** | `node --version` | Required for CodeGraph — v18 or lower will fail silently |
| **uv** | `uv --version` | Python package manager — Claude uses this for all Python operations when the plugin is active |
| **An agent project** | — | The project whose prompts/docs you want to improve |

> [!tip] Python Environment
> When the plugin is active, Claude automatically uses `uv` for all Python work — package installs, script execution, and virtual environment creation. Install `uv` before your first session:
> - macOS / Linux: `curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh` (then restart your shell)
> - Cross-platform: `pip install uv`
>
> Claude will create an isolated `.venv/` in your project root and never touch system Python.

---

## Step 1 — Clone the Repository

```bash
git clone https://gitlab.com/konfio/organization/tribes/data/data-science/behavioral-and-internal-ml/nlp/conversational-ai/experiments-and-development/internal-ops/kandalf.git
cd kandalf
```

> **Existing users**: If you obtained the plugin from another source, update your remote:
> ```bash
> git remote set-url origin https://gitlab.com/konfio/organization/tribes/data/data-science/behavioral-and-internal-ml/nlp/conversational-ai/experiments-and-development/internal-ops/kandalf.git
> ```

---

## Step 2 — Install and Initialize

Run the install script from your agent project root:

```bash
bash kandalf/install.sh
```

This single command handles everything:
- Removes `kandalf/.git` to prevent accidental plugin commits
- Checks that Node.js 20+ is available
- Builds the CodeGraph index for **your working project** (runs `codegraph init -i` from your project root automatically — via `npx -y @colbymchenry/codegraph` when the CLI isn't installed globally)

The CodeGraph and Context7 MCP servers ship with the plugin (`kandalf/.mcp.json`) and start automatically when the plugin loads — no separate MCP install, no Claude Code restart.

**If the script fails**, the error message includes the exact manual command to run. Common issue:
```bash
node --version   # must be 20+ — if not, install from https://nodejs.org
```

---

## Step 3 — Load the Plugin

From your agent project root, load kandalf as a Claude Code plugin:

```bash
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
```

> This loads the plugin for the current session only. Run this command each time you start a new kandalf session. No files are copied to `.claude/` — the plugin loads directly from the `kandalf/` directory.

---

## First Session Walkthrough

> See also: [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]] — a complete worked walkthrough from start to report with sample inputs and outputs at every step.

Once the plugin is loaded, you're ready to start:

```
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs
```

Replace `./docs` with the path to your agent project's documentation directory.

**What happens next**:

1. **Input ingestion** — kandalf reads your docs directory recursively
2. **Discovery Q&A** — an interactive session to define your improvement goals, scope, and constraints
3. **Task plan** — improvement work is decomposed into atomic tasks with CodeGraph impact analysis
4. **Confirmation** — you review and approve the task plan
5. **Execution** — tasks run in dependency order with checkpoints and rollback safety

**Minimum viable invocation**:
```
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs
```

**Full options**:
```
/kandalf:start
  --docs <path>              Required. Path to agent docs directory.
  --project <path>           Optional. High-level project description file.
  --examples <dir>           Optional. Conversation example files.
  --requirements <path>      Optional. Requirements or user-story document.
  --budget-threshold <pct>   Optional. Shutdown threshold % (default: 80).
  --budget-limit <tokens>    Optional. Total token budget (default: 100000).
```

---

## Verification Checklist

After completing all steps, confirm:

- [ ] `codegraph --version` runs without error
- [ ] `.codegraph/` directory exists in your agent project root
- [ ] `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf` loads without errors
- [ ] `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs` starts the Discovery Q&A

---

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Likely Cause | Fix |
|---------|-------------|-----|
| `Warning: CodeGraph index initialization failed` | Node.js < 20 or npx error | Check `node --version`; run `codegraph init -i` from your project root |
| Skills not visible after loading | Plugin not loaded | Re-run `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf` (or `/reload-plugins`) |
| `CodeGraph: ⚠ not initialized` | `install.sh` not run or ran from wrong location | Re-run `bash kandalf/install.sh` from your agent project root |
| `Error: memory.md already exists` | Session in progress | Use `/kandalf:resume` |

See [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting Guide]] for detailed resolutions.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/02-end-to-end-guide.md
==============================
---
title: "End-to-End Example Guide"
tags: [kandalf, guide, end-to-end, tutorial, walkthrough]
aliases: [tutorial, walkthrough, getting started guide]
slug: end-to-end-guide
---

# End-to-End Example Guide

> A complete worked walkthrough: from loading the plugin to generating your first improvement report.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[01-installation|Installation Guide]] · [[commands/01-start|/kandalf:start]] · [[commands/02-resume|/kandalf:resume]] · [[commands/05-report|/kandalf:report]]

---

This guide walks through a full kandalf session using the **Customer Support Bot** project (`kandalf/tests/fixtures/sample-docs/`) as the example. By the end you will have run a complete improvement session and generated a report.

> [!tip] First time?
> Complete the [[01-installation|Installation Guide]] before this guide. You need: `bash kandalf/install.sh` run from your agent project root (handles CodeGraph MCP install and project initialization), and Claude Code restarted.

---

## 1. Load the Plugin

From the root directory of the project you want to improve, load kandalf:

```bash
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
```

Expected output in Claude Code:

```
Plugin loaded: kandalf (6 skills)
  /kandalf:start
  /kandalf:resume
  /kandalf:status
  /kandalf:rollback
  /kandalf:report
  /kandalf:update-docs
```

> The plugin loads for the current session only. Run this command each time you start a new session.

---

## 2. Start a Session

```
/kandalf:start --docs ./kandalf/tests/fixtures/sample-docs
```

kandalf reads every file in the docs directory and then begins the Discovery Q&A. For larger projects, also pass `--project` and `--examples`:

```
/kandalf:start \
  --docs ./agent/docs \
  --project ./agent/project.md \
  --examples ./agent/conversations \
  --budget-threshold 75
```

### Sample Discovery Q&A

kandalf asks a series of focused questions to understand your improvement goals. Example exchange:

---

**kandalf**: What specific behavior do you want to improve in the Customer Support Bot?

**You**: The agent's tone is too formal and robotic. Users have also reported it sometimes fails to use the lookup tool and instead makes up account information.

---

**kandalf**: Which components are in scope for this session?

**You**: The system prompt and the tool call instructions. Leave the few-shot examples alone.

---

**kandalf**: How will you know the improvements worked?

**You**: A human evaluator should rate at least 80% of responses as "natural sounding". Zero instances of fabricated account data in test conversations.

---

**kandalf**: Are there any constraints — things that must not change?

**You**: The escalation protocol at the end of the system prompt must stay exactly as written.

---

### After Discovery Q&A — memory.md Config

After you complete Discovery Q&A and kandalf registers all tasks, `memory.md` is created. Its Config section looks like this:

```
## Config

| Key | Value |
|-----|-------|
| session_id | S-20260526-141022 |
| project_goal | Improve tone naturalness and eliminate tool-use failures in the Customer Support Bot |
| scope | system prompt, tool call instructions |
| docs_path | ./kandalf/tests/fixtures/sample-docs |
| budget_limit | 100000 |
| budget_threshold_pct | 80 |
| created_at | 2026-05-26T14:10:22Z |
| last_docs_update | null |
```

---

## 3. Review and Approve the Task Plan

kandalf shows you the complete task plan before executing anything:

```
Proposed task plan (4 tasks):

T-001  analysis  Analyze system prompt tone patterns             est. 3000 tokens
T-002  analysis  Analyze tool call instruction gaps              est. 2000 tokens
T-003  change    Rewrite system prompt tone section              est. 8000 tokens
                 CodeGraph impact: 3 symbols across 1 file
T-004  change    Strengthen tool call instructions               est. 6000 tokens
                 CodeGraph impact: 2 symbols across 1 file

Total estimated: ~19000 tokens (19% of 100000 budget)

Proceed with this plan? (yes/no)
```

Type `yes` to begin execution.

---

## 4. Task Execution

kandalf executes tasks in dependency order. Analysis tasks run first, then change tasks.

### Example: T-003 Completing

```
▶ T-003 — Rewrite system prompt tone section
  Type: change | File: kandalf/tests/fixtures/sample-docs/system-prompt.md
  CodeGraph impact: systemPrompt (system-prompt.md), toneGuidance (system-prompt.md), supportPersona (system-prompt.md)
  Checkpoint: T-003-step1-S-20260526-141022
  ✅ T-003 complete — system-prompt.md updated
  Budget used: ~31000 / 100000 tokens (31%)
```

Sub-agents execute change tasks using the [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|10-step CodeGraph data flow]] and [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|6-step checkpoint protocol]] — every write is atomic and reversible.

---

## 5. Budget Interruption

If token usage approaches the threshold (default: 80%), kandalf completes the current atomic step and then shuts down gracefully. The output looks like this:

```
⚠️  Budget threshold reached (81% of 100000 estimated tokens used).
Completing current atomic step...

── Session Handoff Summary ──────────────────────────────────
Session:    S-20260526-141022
Completed:  T-001 (analysis), T-002 (analysis), T-003 (change)
Interrupted: T-004 (change) — interrupted at step 2 of 6
Remaining:  T-004 (pending resume)

To continue:  /kandalf:resume
To inspect:   /kandalf:status
To undo T-003: /kandalf:rollback T-003
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
```

**Reading the Handoff Summary**:
- **Completed** — tasks that finished fully; their changes are written and checkpointed
- **Interrupted** — the task that was running when the budget was hit; paused at a checkpoint (step 2 of 6 means 2 of 6 checkpoint steps completed — no file was partially written)
- **Remaining** — tasks that haven't started yet
- **To continue** — the exact command to pick up where you left off

---

## 6. Resume the Session

```
/kandalf:resume
```

kandalf reads `memory.md`, detects the interrupted task, and presents your options:

```
Interrupted task: T-004 — Strengthen tool call instructions
  Status: interrupted
  Last checkpoint: T-004-step2-S-20260526-141022
  Last step: 2
  Next action: Continue from step 3 — write new content to tmp/ and rename to target
  Options: (a) Resume from checkpoint  (b) Roll back to pre-task state  (c) Skip (defer)

Your choice:
```

Type `a` to resume from where it left off. The task continues from step 3.

```
▶ T-004 resumed from checkpoint (step 3 of 6)
  ✅ T-004 complete — tool call instructions updated
  Budget used: ~87000 / 100000 tokens (87%)

All tasks complete.
Run /kandalf:report to generate your improvement report.
```

---

## 7. Generate the Report

```
/kandalf:report
```

Sample report excerpt:

```markdown
# kandalf Improvement Report

**Project**: Improve tone naturalness and eliminate tool-use failures in the Customer Support Bot
**Sessions**: 2 | **Tasks Completed**: 4 | **Files Modified**: 2
**Generated**: 2026-05-26T15:22:00Z

---

## Baseline Assessment

| Metric | Value |
|--------|-------|
| Symbols at session start | 12 |
| Current symbols | 14 |
| CodeGraph backend | native |

---

## Changes Applied

### T-003: Rewrite system prompt tone section

**What changed**: system-prompt.md — rewrote tone guidance from formal to conversational
**Impact radius** (CodeGraph): 3 symbols across 1 file
**Before**: "You are a helpful customer support agent... Be helpful and concise."
**After**: "You're here to help customers quickly and warmly. Use plain language..."

### T-004: Strengthen tool call instructions

**What changed**: system-prompt.md — added mandatory tool-use rule before answering account questions
**Impact radius** (CodeGraph): 2 symbols across 1 file

---

## Delta Summary

| Metric | Before | After | Change |
|--------|--------|-------|--------|
| Symbols indexed | 12 | 14 | +2 |
| Files modified | 0 | 2 | +2 |
| Tasks completed | 0 | 4 | +4 |

---

## Recommended Next Steps

All improvement tasks completed.
Consider running /kandalf:update-docs to sync your documentation with the changes made.
```

**Interpreting the metrics**:
- **Tasks Completed**: Number of `change` tasks fully applied (analysis and report tasks are not counted)
- **Files Modified**: Unique files changed across all completed tasks
- **Symbols indexed**: Tracked by CodeGraph — an increase means the codebase became more structured after the improvements
- **Impact radius**: How many symbols in the codebase were affected by each change; higher numbers mean wider blast radius

---

## 8. Update Documentation After the Session

Once you've made changes to your agent project, keep its documentation in sync:

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./agent/docs
```

This scans your docs directory, uses CodeGraph to identify sections that reference changed symbols, and updates outdated content. Run it after any improvement session to prevent documentation drift.

To preview changes before applying them:

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./agent/docs --dry-run
```

To also fill sparse or missing sections:

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./agent/docs --enrich
```

See [[commands/06-update-docs|/kandalf:update-docs]] for full parameter reference.

---

## 9. Next Steps

You've completed a full kandalf session. Here's where to go next:

**Command reference** — deep-dive into any command:
- [[commands/01-start|/kandalf:start]] — all parameters and output formats
- [[commands/02-resume|/kandalf:resume]] — interrupt handling and options
- [[commands/03-status|/kandalf:status]] — reading the task dashboard
- [[commands/04-rollback|/kandalf:rollback]] — undoing specific tasks
- [[commands/05-report|/kandalf:report]] — report options and structure
- [[commands/06-update-docs|/kandalf:update-docs]] — documentation sync and enrichment

**Concepts** — understand the internals:
- [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] — the full state file structure
- [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] — how atomic writes work
- [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] — the 10-step data flow
- [[concepts/04-budget-detection|Budget Detection]] — how token estimation works
- [[concepts/05-sub-agent-model|Sub-Agent Model]] — how parallel execution is coordinated

📂 FILE: ./docs/06-state-files.md
