---
title: "State Files Reference"
tags: [kandalf, reference, state-files, directory-layout]
aliases: [.kandalf, directory layout]
slug: state-files
---

# State Files Reference

> Complete layout of files created and managed by kandalf.

See also: [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] · [[00-index|Index]]

---

## Project Root Files

```
your-agent-project/
├── memory.md              # Single source of truth for all session state
├── knowledge/             # Knowledge registry: durable cross-session learnings
│   ├── index.md           # Entries grouped by type
│   ├── timeline.md        # Reverse-chronological session sections
│   └── entries/           # One atomic note per entry (K-NNN-slug.md)
└── .kandalf/        # Runtime state directory
    ├── checkpoints/       # Checkpoint JSON files
    ├── rollbacks/         # Original file copies (pre-change)
    ├── sessions/          # Full session traces
    └── tmp/               # Staging area (auto-cleaned on success)
```

---

## `memory.md`

Location: **project root** (not inside `kandalf/`)

The session state file. Contains 10 required sections: Config, Project Goal, Scope, Budget & Quota Tracking, Task Registry, Task Details, Change Log, Session Log, Checkpoint Registry, and Open Questions.

See [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] for full field definitions and validation rules.

**Commit or `.gitignore`**: Your choice. Committing it preserves state across machine changes; ignoring it keeps the repo clean.

---

## `knowledge/`

Location: **project root** (path configurable via `knowledge_path` in `memory.md` Config; default `knowledge/`)

The knowledge registry — an Obsidian-compatible vault of durable cross-session learnings: decisions, successes, failures (with retry conditions), constraints, and insights. Created by `/kandalf:start` (the only command allowed to create it); read and written by every command through the operations in `lib/knowledge-protocol.md`.

Unlike `memory.md` session state, the registry is **never archived** — entries persist for the life of the project (superseded entries are marked, never deleted).

See [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] for the entry schema, lifecycle integration, and supersession semantics.

**Commit or `.gitignore`**: Your choice. Committing it shares project knowledge with your team.

---

## `.kandalf/checkpoints/`

One JSON file per checkpoint:

```
.kandalf/checkpoints/T-002-step1-S-20260521-093045.json
```

Each file contains: checkpoint ID, task ID, step index, session ID, the original file content (used for restore), and the CodeGraph snapshot from the impact analysis.

Used by `/kandalf:resume` to restore file state when resuming an interrupted task.

See [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol#step-3|Checkpoint Protocol Step 3]] for the JSON schema.

---

## `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/`

One directory per task, created when the task's first `change` step runs:

```
.kandalf/rollbacks/T-002/prompts__system.md
.kandalf/rollbacks/T-003/config__tools.json
```

**Path encoding**: `/` → `__` and `:` → `__colon__` in filenames.

The first copy is the true pre-kandalf original. If a file is modified by the same task in multiple steps, the rollback copy is never overwritten.

Used by `/kandalf:rollback` to restore files.

---

## `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json`

One JSON file per completed or interrupted session:

```
.kandalf/sessions/S-20260521-093045.json
```

Contains the full execution trace for the session: all tasks claimed, all sub-agent activities, all CodeGraph calls, and final state.

Written at the end of every session (normal close or graceful shutdown).

---

## `.kandalf/tmp/`

Staging area for in-progress writes:

```
.kandalf/tmp/prompts__system.md.tmp
```

Files appear here only during [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol#step-4|Checkpoint Protocol Step 4]] (write to tmp) and disappear after [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol#step-5|Step 5]] (rename to target).

If `.tmp` files are found on session start, kandalf detects them as incomplete operations and offers to apply or discard them. The directory is auto-cleaned after a successful session close.

---

## `kandalf/` Plugin Directory

The plugin itself lives in `kandalf/` inside your project. It is not modified at runtime; it provides commands, library references, and templates.

```
kandalf/
├── .claude-plugin/
│   └── plugin.json              # Official plugin manifest (name, description, version, author)
├── install.sh                   # CodeGraph setup helper
├── README.md                    # Quick-start reference
├── docs/                        # This Obsidian vault
├── skills/                      # One SKILL.md per command (loaded as /kandalf:<name>)
│   ├── start/SKILL.md
│   ├── resume/SKILL.md
│   ├── status/SKILL.md
│   ├── rollback/SKILL.md
│   └── report/SKILL.md
├── lib/                         # Protocol and schema references
│   ├── memory-schema.md
│   ├── checkpoint-protocol.md
│   ├── codegraph-integration.md
│   └── budget-detection.md
├── templates/                   # Initial content templates
│   ├── memory-template.md
│   └── session-handoff-template.md
└── tests/                       # Integration and contract tests
    ├── integration/
    └── contract/
```

**Load with**: `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf`

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/04-workflow.md
==============================
---
title: "Complete Workflow Guide"
tags: [kandalf, workflow, lifecycle, session]
aliases: [session lifecycle, workflow guide, how it works]
slug: workflow
---

# kandalf Workflow Guide

> Complete session lifecycle from initial start to final report.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/08-execute|execute]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[commands/05-report|report]] · [[commands/06-update-docs|update-docs]] · [[commands/07-iterate|iterate]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]]

---

## Overview

> For a complete worked example with real sample inputs and outputs at each step, see [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]].

A kandalf session moves through six phases, with optional iterate and documentation sync steps:

```
[1] Discovery  →  [2] Edge Case Q&A  →  [3] Task Plan  →  [4] Execute (gate)  →  [5] Execution  →  [6] Resilience  →  [7] Report  →  [8] Iterate (optional)  →  [9] Sync Docs (optional)
```

**`/kandalf:start`** covers phases 1–3 and stops. **`/kandalf:execute`** covers phases 5–6.

Each phase writes state to [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md]], ensuring full resumability at any point.

---

## Phase 1 — Discovery

**Command**: `/kandalf:start --docs <path> [options]`

1. **Input ingestion**: The plugin reads all files in your `--docs` directory (recursively), plus any optional `--project`, `--examples`, and `--requirements` files. All text formats are accepted.

2. **CodeGraph check**: Verifies `.codegraph/` exists in your project root. If not, it prompts you to re-run `bash kandalf/install.sh` (which initializes CodeGraph automatically). Once the index is ready, baseline metrics are captured (symbol count, sync time, backend).

3. **Discovery Q&A**: An interactive session with you to establish:
   - What specific behaviors need improvement (accuracy, tone, tool usage, etc.)
   - Which components are in scope (system prompts, tool definitions, few-shot examples, code files)
   - Success criteria — how will you know improvements worked
   - Constraints — what must not change

4. **memory.md creation**: The session ID, project goal, scope, and CodeGraph baseline are written to `memory.md` at the project root. **No task is registered and no file is touched until memory.md is fully written.**

**State at end of Phase 1**:
- `memory.md` exists with Config, Project Goal, Scope, and empty Task Registry
- `.kandalf/` directory structure created
- Session Log row: `active`

---

## Phase 2 — Task Plan

Still within `/kandalf:start`.

1. **Task decomposition**: Based on Discovery Q&A, the plugin decomposes all improvement work into atomic tasks. Each task gets:
   - A unique `T-NNN` ID
   - A type: `analysis`, `change`, `validation`, or `report`
   - Dependencies on other tasks
   - A parallelizability flag

2. **CodeGraph impact pre-analysis**: For every `change` task, `codegraph_impact` is called on the primary symbol/file. The blast radius is logged to the task's `CodeGraph Impact` field before execution begins. Tasks with impact radius > 20 symbols trigger a confirmation prompt.

3. **Task registration**: Every task is appended to both the Task Registry table and a Task Details subsection in `memory.md` **before** execution starts.

4. **User confirmation**: The full task plan is shown to you. Execution begins only after your approval.

**State at end of Phase 2**:
- All tasks registered in `memory.md` with status `pending`
- CodeGraph impact data logged for all `change` tasks
- User has approved the plan

---

## Phase 3 — Execution

kandalf executes tasks in dependency order.

### For each task:

1. Task status → `in_progress`, Owner → current session ID
2. Sub-agent spawned (or inline execution for small analysis tasks)
3. Sub-agent follows [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|10-step CodeGraph data flow]] for `change` tasks
4. Sub-agent follows [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|6-step checkpoint protocol]] for every file write
5. After completion: task verified against its Success Criteria
6. If pass → status `completed`; if fail → status stays `in_progress` with retry note
7. [[concepts/04-budget-detection|Budget check]] after each task

### Parallelizable tasks

Tasks with no overlapping output files run concurrently via sub-agents. Each sub-agent:
- Reads `memory.md` to check file ownership before touching any file
- Writes results back to `memory.md` on completion

### Single task with multiple sub-agents

A single task may spawn multiple coordinated sub-agents (e.g., one to analyze, one to write). The parent session coordinates their work; file ownership rules prevent conflicts.

**State progression during Phase 3**:
- Task Registry rows transition: `pending` → `in_progress` → `completed`
- Change Log grows with `planned` → `applied` entries
- Checkpoint Registry grows with each file write
- `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` populated before each file is touched

---

## Phase 4 — Resilience

Budget exhaustion, quota limits, or `Ctrl-C` trigger a graceful shutdown — the session **does not lose work**.

### Graceful shutdown sequence:

1. Current atomic step completes (never abandoned mid-write)
2. In-progress task gets a checkpoint saved
3. Task status → `interrupted`; Owner cleared
4. Session Log → End Time + outcome (`interrupted-budget`, `interrupted-user`, etc.)
5. Session Handoff Summary written to `memory.md` and output to screen
6. Full session trace written to `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json`

### Resuming:

```
/kandalf:resume
```

1. Reads and validates `memory.md`
2. Shows interrupted tasks with their last checkpoint and next step
3. You choose per-task: resume from checkpoint, roll back to pre-task state, or defer
4. Pending tasks listed; you select which to execute this session
5. New session ID generated; execution continues from where it stopped

See [[commands/02-resume]] for full details.

---

## Phase 5 — Report

```
/kandalf:report
```

Generates a structured before/after report:

- **Baseline Assessment**: CodeGraph metrics at session start vs. current
- **Per-change sections**: For each completed `change` task — what changed, before/after snippets, CodeGraph impact radius, and a concrete test example
- **Delta Summary**: Table of metrics before and after all changes
- **Recommended Next Steps**: Remaining `pending` or `deferred` tasks

See [[commands/05-report]] for output format and options.

---

## Phase 6 — Sync Documentation (Optional)

After changes are made to your agent project, keep its documentation in sync:

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs
```

This command uses CodeGraph to detect which doc sections reference symbols that changed since the last update and rewrites those sections. Use `--enrich` to also fill sparse or empty sections. Use `--dry-run` to preview without writing.

See [[commands/06-update-docs]] for full parameter reference.

---

## State Transitions Diagram

```
             /kandalf:start
                     │
                     ▼
         [Discovery Q&A + Edge Case Q&A]
                     │
                     ▼
            [Task Registration]  ← all tasks registered in memory.md
                     │                (including edge case tasks)
                     ▼
          ┌──────────────────────┐
          │  Review & Decide     │  ← /kandalf:start stops here
          │                      │
          │  execute → run plan  │
          │  iterate → refine    │
          │  status  → review    │
          └──────────┬───────────┘
                     │ /kandalf:execute
                     ▼
    ┌────────────────┴──────────────────┐
    │         Task Execution Loop       │
    │                                   │
    │  pending → in_progress            │
    │      ↓           ↓               │
    │  blocked     completed            │
    │                  ↓               │
    │          (budget check)          │
    │            ↓         ↓           │
    │      threshold    OK → next task  │
    │      exceeded                     │
    │          ↓                        │
    │   interrupted                     │
    │   (graceful shutdown)             │
    └───────────────────────────────────┘
                     │
          all tasks completed           OR   mixed statuses (mid-session)
                     │                                │
                     ▼                                │
            /kandalf:report                     │
                     │                                │
                     └──────────┬─────────────────────┘
                                │
                     (optional) ▼
                  /kandalf:iterate
                     │  Refinement conversation
                     │  → update goal/scope
                     │  → register new pending tasks
                     │  → defer superseded pending tasks
                     │
                     ▼
            /kandalf:resume  ← executes new pending tasks
```

---

## Complete Command Flow

```
/kandalf:start --docs ./agent-docs
    → Discovery Q&A
    → Task plan confirmation
    → Execution (with CodeGraph impact checks on every change)
    → Optional: budget hit → graceful shutdown

/kandalf:status
    → Review task registry, budget, CodeGraph health

/kandalf:resume
    → Resolve interrupted tasks
    → Continue execution

/kandalf:rollback T-003
    → Preview files to restore
    → Confirm → restore pre-task state
    → codegraph sync

/kandalf:report
    → Before/after comparison
    → Impact radius per change
    → Test examples
    → Delta summary

/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs
    → Detect changed symbols since last update
    → Update outdated doc sections
    → Optional: enrich sparse sections (--enrich)

/kandalf:iterate
    → Validate memory.md (same rules as start/resume)
    → Display current goal, scope, task summary
    → Conversational requirement refinement
    → Propose Requirement Delta (new tasks, deferrals, goal/scope changes)
    → Confirm → write to memory.md
    → Run /kandalf:resume to execute new tasks

/kandalf:iterate --scope "tool definitions"
    → Same as above, filtered to tasks/requirements matching "tool definitions"

/kandalf:execute
    → Validate memory.md (all 5 rules)
    → Check for interrupted tasks → redirect to /kandalf:resume if found
    → Check for locked (in_progress) tasks → warn + confirm
    → Check for zero pending tasks → report all-complete + suggest next steps
    → Show execution wave plan
    → Execute tasks in dependency order (checkpoint protocol, CodeGraph impact, budget monitoring)
    → On completion: "Run /kandalf:report · /kandalf:iterate · /kandalf:update-docs"
    → On interruption: "Run /kandalf:resume · /kandalf:status · /kandalf:report"
```

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/08-permissions.md
==============================
# Kandalf Pre-Approved Tool Permissions

Kandalf ships with a `permissions.allow` list in `.claude/settings.local.json` that pre-approves every tool needed for a routine session. This eliminates Claude Code permission dialogs during normal operation while keeping destructive operations protected.

---

## Pre-Approved Tools

| Entry | Category | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| `Read` | File I/O | Reads spec files, SKILL.md, lib/, docs/ during session work |
| `Edit` | File I/O | Edits memory.md, task files, and source files under improvement |
| `Write` | File I/O | Creates new files when implementing Kandalf tasks |
| `Bash(codegraph *)` | Analysis | Runs CodeGraph CLI for structural code analysis |
| `Bash(npx *)` | Analysis | Runs npx-based tools (e.g. tree-sitter parsers) |
| `Bash(node *)` | Analysis | Runs Node.js scripts for analysis tasks |
| `Bash(git status)` | Git (read-only) | Shows working tree state |
| `Bash(git log *)` | Git (read-only) | Reads commit history |
| `Bash(git diff *)` | Git (read-only) | Shows diffs between commits or working tree |
| `Bash(git show *)` | Git (read-only) | Shows commit content |
| `Bash(git branch *)` | Git (read-only) | Lists or inspects branches |
| `Bash(ls)` | Navigation | Lists current directory |
| `Bash(ls *)` | Navigation | Lists a specific directory |
| `Bash(find *)` | Navigation | Finds files by name or pattern |
| `Bash(cat *)` | Navigation | Reads file content inline |
| `Bash(grep *)` | Search | Searches file contents for patterns |
| `Bash(wc *)` | Analysis | Counts lines/words/bytes in files |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_status` | CodeGraph MCP | Checks index health |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_search` | CodeGraph MCP | Finds symbols by name |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_context` | CodeGraph MCP | Gets focused context for a task area |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_node` | CodeGraph MCP | Shows a symbol's source/signature |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_callers` | CodeGraph MCP | Finds callers of a symbol |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_callees` | CodeGraph MCP | Finds callees of a symbol |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_impact` | CodeGraph MCP | Estimates blast radius of a change |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_explore` | CodeGraph MCP | Deep-dives an unfamiliar module |
| `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_files` | CodeGraph MCP | Lists files under a path |
| `mcp__plugin_context7_context7__resolve-library-id` | Context7 MCP | Resolves a library name to a Context7 ID |
| `mcp__plugin_context7_context7__query-docs` | Context7 MCP | Fetches up-to-date library documentation |

---

## What Is NOT Pre-Approved

The following operations always require explicit user confirmation:

| Pattern | Reason |
|---|---|
| `Bash(rm *)` | File deletion is irreversible |
| `Bash(git commit *)` | Commits change project history |
| `Bash(git push *)` | Pushes changes to a remote — affects shared state |
| `Bash(git reset *)` | Can rewrite or destroy history |
| `Bash(sudo *)` | Privilege escalation — high blast radius |
| `Bash(mv *)` | Moving/renaming files is hard to undo without git |
| Bare `Bash` (no pattern) | Blanket allow is too broad; specific patterns only |
| Any tool not in the list above | Deny by default |

---

## Customizing the List

The `permissions.allow` list is defined in `kandalf/.claude/settings.local.json`. To add or remove entries:

1. Open `kandalf/.claude/settings.local.json`
2. Edit the `permissions.allow` array
3. Use the same string format:
   - Plain tool name: `"Read"`, `"Edit"`, `"Write"`
   - Bash with glob: `"Bash(git log *)"`, `"Bash(find *)"`
   - MCP tool: `"mcp__<server>__<tool>"`
4. Save the file — changes take effect on the next session start

**To add a new tool** (example: allow `npm run test`):
```json
"Bash(npm run test)"
```

**To remove a tool** (example: stop auto-approving `Write`): delete its entry from the array.

**To tighten a pattern** (example: only allow `git log` on main, not all branches): replace `"Bash(git log *)"` with `"Bash(git log main)"`.

> Changes to this file are tracked in git. Review additions carefully before committing — entries in `permissions.allow` run without a confirmation dialog.

# 🧙‍♂️ Kandalf
> "A wizard never gives the wrong output. Nor does he hallucinate. He generates precisely what he means to..." 🪄✨

**Agent engineering harness for developing AI-agent projects, specialized in improving conversational AI agents**

kandalf is a Claude Code plugin that systematically analyzes, improves, and validates conversational agent projects. It conducts a structured discovery session, decomposes improvement goals into atomic tasks, executes changes via coordinated sub-agents with full CodeGraph impact analysis, and produces a quantitative improvement report — all with budget resilience, checkpoint-based resumability, and per-task rollback.

---

> [!tip] New here? Start with Installation
> Run `bash kandalf/install.sh` from your agent project root — it builds the CodeGraph index for your project; the CodeGraph and Context7 MCP servers (plus the caveman and Obsidian markdown skills) ship with the plugin, nothing else to install. Load the plugin with `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf` and everything starts with `/kandalf:start --docs ./docs`.
> Open [[architecture.canvas|Architecture Canvas]] or [[session-flow.canvas|Session Flow Canvas]] in Obsidian for a visual overview.

---

## Getting Started

- [[01-installation|Installation Guide]] — clone, install CodeGraph, and run your first session
- [[02-end-to-end-guide|End-to-End Example Guide]] — complete worked walkthrough from start to report
- [[03-use-guide|Use Guide]] — all commands at a glance with common scenarios
- [[04-workflow|Complete Workflow Guide]] — full session lifecycle narrative
- Commands → [[commands/01-start|/kandalf:start]] — begin here

## Command Reference

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| [[commands/01-start\|/kandalf:start]] | Start a new improvement session |
| [[commands/02-resume\|/kandalf:resume]] | Resume an interrupted session |
| [[commands/03-status\|/kandalf:status]] | Show task registry and session summary |
| [[commands/04-rollback\|/kandalf:rollback]] | Roll back task changes |
| [[commands/05-report\|/kandalf:report]] | Generate the improvement report |
| [[commands/06-update-docs\|/kandalf:update-docs]] | Sync documentation with current codebase using CodeGraph |

## Concepts

| Concept | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| [[concepts/01-memory-schema\|memory.md Schema]] | The single source of truth for all session state — including the Archive Index and registration-time integrity rules (7–9) |
| [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol\|Checkpoint Protocol]] | 6-step atomic write and rollback safety system, with per-step failure cleanup and the O1–O5 orphan recovery scan |
| [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration\|CodeGraph Integration]] | Mandatory impact analysis before every file change |
| [[concepts/04-budget-detection\|Budget Detection]] | Token estimation and graceful shutdown algorithm |
| [[concepts/05-sub-agent-model\|Sub-Agent Model]] | How kandalf coordinates parallel sub-agents |
| [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry\|Knowledge Registry]] | Durable cross-session learnings in an Obsidian-compatible `knowledge/` vault — captured on completion/rollback, consulted at planning, never deleted |
| [[concepts/07-compression-policy\|Compression Policy (caveman)]] | Dynamic caveman output compression — full for simple work, lite for complex, off for planning and artifacts; default-on with one-step opt-out |
| [[concepts/08-context7-lookup\|Context7 Documentation Lookup]] | Current official docs consulted before any change that depends on an external library API — bundled MCP server, capped wait, explicit disclosure |

> [!note] Shared rules & session archiving
> Common rules (validation checklist, ID formats, dual-update convention) live in `lib/common-preamble.md` — every command reads it first and loads only the additional references it needs (see the [[05-architecture|Architecture Guide]] reference-loading matrix). Completed sessions are archived to `.kandalf/archive/` per `lib/archive-protocol.md`, keeping `memory.md` bounded on long projects.

- [[architecture.canvas|Architecture Canvas]] — interactive component overview (open in Obsidian)

## Installation Quick-Reference

```bash
# 1. Clone into your agent project root
git clone https://gitlab.com/konfio/organization/tribes/data/data-science/behavioral-and-internal-ml/nlp/conversational-ai/experiments-and-development/internal-ops/kandalf.git

# 2. Install (builds the CodeGraph index; MCP servers ship with the plugin)
bash kandalf/install.sh

# 3. Load the plugin and start
claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf
# /kandalf:start --docs <path-to-your-agent-docs>
```

# Troubleshooting Guide

> Common errors and how to resolve them.

See also: [[00-index|Index]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]]

---

## Installation Issues

### `Warning: CodeGraph index initialization failed. Continuing without impact analysis.`

**Cause**: `codegraph init -i` failed during `/kandalf:start` (the MCP server itself ships with the plugin via `kandalf/.mcp.json` and needs no install).

**Resolution**:
1. Check Node.js version: `node --version` — must be 20+
2. From your project root (outside the kandalf folder), run `codegraph init -i` (or `npx -y @colbymchenry/codegraph init -i`)

**Note**: kandalf continues without CodeGraph if init fails, but `change` tasks will not have impact analysis, and the report will lack CodeGraph metrics.

---

### `CodeGraph: ⚠ not initialized`

**Cause**: CodeGraph is installed as an MCP server but the index has not been built for this project.

**Resolution**: From your project root (outside the kandalf folder), run `codegraph install --yes`, then restart Claude Code.

---

### Skills not showing up after install

**Cause**: SKILL.md files were installed but Claude Code was not restarted.

**Resolution**: Restart Claude Code. Skills are loaded at startup.

---

## Session Issues

### `Error: memory.md already exists. Run /kandalf:resume to continue...`

**Cause**: A previous session is still in progress.

**Resolution**: 
- Use `/kandalf:resume` to continue the existing session
- Use `/kandalf:status` to see what tasks remain
- To start completely fresh: run `/kandalf:rollback --all`, then delete `memory.md` manually

---

### `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to begin a new session.`

**Cause**: No active session exists.

**Resolution**: Run `/kandalf:start --docs <path>` to start a new session.

---

### `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...`

**Cause**: The state file is corrupted or manually edited incorrectly.

**Resolution**: kandalf offers two options:
- **(a) Reset to last checkpoint**: Restores the most recent entry in Checkpoint Registry. Any tasks completed after that checkpoint are lost but can be re-executed.
- **(b) Archive and start fresh**: Renames `memory.md` to `memory.md.corrupted.{timestamp}`. You start a new session. The archived file is preserved for manual inspection.

---

### Session hangs or does not resume

**Cause**: A stale `in_progress` task owned by a previous session ID.

**Resolution**: Run `/kandalf:resume`. It automatically detects stale locks (tasks with `in_progress` status and an owner that does not match the current session) and presents them as interrupted tasks with the three resolution options (resume / roll back / defer).

---

## Task Execution Issues

### Task stuck at `in_progress` with no output

**Cause**: A sub-agent was spawned but the Claude Code session ended before it completed.

**Resolution**: 
1. Run `/kandalf:resume`
2. The stuck task will appear as a stale-lock interrupted task
3. Choose (a) to resume from the last checkpoint, or (b) to roll back

---

### `Error: rollback files for T-NNN not found in .kandalf/rollbacks/.`

**Cause**: The task's rollback directory is missing (possibly deleted manually).

**Resolution**: 
- Check `memory.md` Change Log for the original content — it records what changed and when
- Restore the file manually from git history if available: `git show HEAD:path/to/file > path/to/file`
- After manual restoration, update the task status to `pending` in `memory.md` and run `codegraph sync`

---

### CodeGraph impact threshold exceeded (21+ symbols)

**Cause**: A `change` task's `codegraph_impact` result showed more than 20 affected symbols. The sub-agent halted and is waiting for confirmation.

**Resolution**: 
- Review the affected symbols listed in the task's CodeGraph Impact field in `memory.md`
- If the blast radius is expected: confirm via `/kandalf:resume` and choose to proceed
- If the blast radius is unexpected: consider splitting the task into smaller changes — roll back if needed and create two replacement tasks in the task plan

---

## Report Issues

### `No completed changes found. Run /kandalf:status...`

**Cause**: No `change` tasks have status `completed`.

**Resolution**: Complete at least one change task. Use `/kandalf:resume` to continue execution, or `/kandalf:status` to see the current task states.

---

### `Error: cannot write to '<path>'. Check permissions.`

**Cause**: The path provided to `--output` is not writable.

**Resolution**: Check file permissions, or use a different output path:
```
/kandalf:report --output ~/Desktop/report.md
```

---

## CodeGraph Issues

### `codegraph status` shows stale index

**Cause**: Files were changed but CodeGraph's auto-sync (2-second debounce) has not completed.

**Resolution**: Wait 3 seconds, then run `codegraph sync` manually if still stale.

---

### `.codegraph/` directory missing after rollback

**Cause**: Rollback restored files to a state before CodeGraph was initialized.

**Resolution**: From your project root, run `codegraph install --yes` again, then `codegraph sync`.

---

## Manual Recovery Checklist

If automated repair options fail, use this checklist:

1. Check `.kandalf/checkpoints/` for the latest checkpoint JSON — it contains the original file content in `checkpoint.files`
2. Check `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` for original file copies
3. Check `memory.md` Change Log for the history of what changed and when
4. Restore files manually from rollback copies or checkpoint JSON
5. Edit `memory.md` to update task statuses to match restored state
6. Run `codegraph sync`
7. Re-invoke `/kandalf:resume`

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol.md
==============================
---
title: "Checkpoint Protocol"
tags: [kandalf, concept, checkpoint, atomic-write, safety, recovery]
aliases: [checkpoint protocol, atomic write, rollback safety]
slug: concepts/checkpoint-protocol
---

# Checkpoint Protocol

> 6-step atomic write and rollback safety system for every file modification.

See also: [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[commands/04-rollback|rollback]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]]

---

## Overview

Every `change` task in kandalf modifies files using this 6-step protocol. Skipping any step risks data loss. The protocol ensures that:

- The original file is always preserved before any change
- A planned-but-not-applied state is detectable and recoverable
- An interrupted write leaves the original file intact
- Any checkpoint can be used to resume or restore
- A failure at any step has a defined cleanup action (per-step Failure Handling table), and every session start runs an O1–O5 orphan recovery scan that auto-resolves all artifact classes except the one genuinely ambiguous case (a complete `.tmp` with a registered checkpoint, which prompts apply-or-discard)

---

## The 6 Steps

### Step 1 — Write Rollback Copy

**Before any other action**, copy the original file to:

```
.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/{encodedPath}
```

**Path encoding**: Replace `/` with `__` and `:` with `__colon__`.

| Original path | Rollback filename |
|--------------|------------------|
| `prompts/system.md` | `prompts__system.md` |
| `config/agent.json` | `config__agent.json` |

**Critical**: If a rollback copy for this file already exists in the task's directory, **do not overwrite it**. The first copy is the true pre-kandalf original.

> [!warning] Never overwrite the first rollback copy
> Overwriting it destroys the ability to fully undo the session. The check `if rollback copy exists → skip` must happen before any write.

**On failure**: Halt the task. Do not proceed to Step 2.

---

### Step 2 — Write Intent to Change Log

Append a `planned` entry to `memory.md` Change Log **before** applying the change:

```
| 2026-05-21T09:44:00Z | T-002 | prompts/system.md | Rewrite tone guidance | planned | Discovery Q&A: overly formal language |
```

This allows the next session to detect a planned-but-not-applied state if the session dies between Step 2 and Step 6.

---

### Step 3 — Write Checkpoint File

Write the checkpoint JSON to:

```
.kandalf/checkpoints/{checkpointId}.json
```

**Checkpoint ID format**: `{taskId}-step{N}-{sessionId}`

```json
{
  "checkpointId": "T-002-step1-S-20260521-093045",
  "taskId": "T-002",
  "stepIndex": 1,
  "sessionId": "S-20260521-093045",
  "description": "About to rewrite tone section in prompts/system.md",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-21T09:44:00Z",
  "files": {
    "prompts/system.md": "<full original file content>"
  },
  "codeGraphSnapshot": {
    "symbol": "systemPrompt",
    "impactRadius": 4,
    "affectedSymbols": ["systemPrompt", "toneConfig"],
    "affectedFiles": ["prompts/system.md", "config/agent.json"],
    "rawOutput": "..."
  },
  "nextStepDescription": "Write new content to tmp/prompts__system.md.tmp and rename to target"
}
```

After writing: add a row to `memory.md` Checkpoint Registry; update the task's `Checkpoint` field in both Task Registry and Task Details.

---

### Step 4 — Write New Content to Temp File

Write the complete new file content to:

```
.kandalf/tmp/{encodedPath}.tmp
```

**Do not** write directly to the target path. If the session dies here, the original file is intact (Step 1 copied it) and the checkpoint exists (Step 3).

---

### Step 5 — Atomic Rename to Target Path

Rename `.kandalf/tmp/{encodedPath}.tmp` to the actual target path.

This is the **only step that modifies the target file**. If the session dies between Step 4 and Step 5, the original is still intact and the `.tmp` file is detectable on next session start.

---

### Step 6 — Update Change Log to Applied

Update the `planned` entry from Step 2 to `applied`:

```
| 2026-05-21T09:44:35Z | T-002 | prompts/system.md | Rewrite tone guidance | applied | Discovery Q&A: overly formal language |
```

Also finalize the task's `CodeGraph Impact` field in Task Details if not already set.

---

## Multi-Step Tasks

Tasks that modify multiple files create one checkpoint per file:

```
T-003-step1-S-...   → before modifying file1
T-003-step2-S-...   → before modifying file2
T-003-step3-S-...   → before modifying file3
```

The task's `Checkpoint` field always points to the **latest** checkpoint.

---

## Detecting Incomplete Operations

On every session start, kandalf scans `.kandalf/tmp/` for any `.tmp` files.

**If found**:
1. Alert: `Found incomplete write operation: {filename}.tmp`
2. Look up the corresponding checkpoint in Checkpoint Registry
3. If checkpoint found: offer to **(a) apply** the planned change or **(b) discard** the `.tmp`
4. If no checkpoint: offer to discard the `.tmp` (orphaned)
5. Clean up `.tmp` after resolution

---

## Checkpoint Restore Protocol (used by `/kandalf:resume`)

To restore state from checkpoint `{checkpointId}`:

1. Read `.kandalf/checkpoints/{checkpointId}.json`
2. For each file in `checkpoint.files`:
   a. Write current file to `.kandalf/tmp/{filename}.tmp` (safety copy)
   b. Write `checkpoint.files[path]` (the original content) to target via rename
3. Update task status to `in_progress` with current session ID in `memory.md`
4. Continue from `checkpoint.stepIndex + 1`

---

## Rollback vs. Checkpoint Restore

| Operation | Source | When to Use |
|-----------|--------|-------------|
| Rollback | `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` | Undo a **completed** task's changes |
| Checkpoint restore | `.kandalf/checkpoints/{checkpointId}.json` | Resume an **interrupted** task from mid-execution |

These are complementary: rollback is for undoing finished work; checkpoint restore is for continuing interrupted work.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/08-context7-lookup.md

---
title: "Context7 Documentation Lookup"
tags: [kandalf, concept, context7, documentation, mcp]
aliases: [context7, docs lookup, documentation lookup]
slug: context7-lookup
---

# Context7 Documentation Lookup

> Tasks that touch external library APIs consult current official documentation before changing anything — so changes match the version that exists today, not the one in training data.

See also: [[03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[00-index|Index]]

Authoritative protocol: `lib/context7-protocol.md`. This page is the concept overview; the lib file wins on any conflict.

---

## What it is

The [Context7](https://github.com/upstash/context7) MCP server ships with the plugin (`kandalf/.mcp.json` — `npx -y @upstash/context7-mcp`, no extra install). When a task's change depends on an external library, framework, SDK, or CLI API, the executing agent:

1. Resolves the library to a Context7 ID.
2. Queries the docs with a task-specific question.
3. Bases the change on the returned documentation.
4. Records `Docs consulted: {library} ({id}) — found.` in the task's Notes.

This is **Step 0** of the change-task data flow ([[03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]]) — it happens before any analysis is logged or file is written.

## Tagging

- At decomposition (`/kandalf:start` Section 7), each task is tagged with its external dependencies (or none).
- At execution, the agent re-checks before the first dependent change — mis-tags are corrected on the spot.
- Untagged (dependency-free) tasks skip lookups entirely: zero added latency.

## When docs can't be retrieved

Capped at 2 attempts / ~10 seconds per library — a slow or offline server never blocks a task. On `not-found` or `unavailable`, the task proceeds on built-in knowledge and discloses exactly:

```
Note: current documentation for '{library}' could not be retrieved — proceeding on built-in knowledge.
```

The outcome lands in the task's Notes either way, so the report can show which changes were docs-backed.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/06-knowledge-registry.md
==============================
---
title: "Knowledge Registry"
tags: [kandalf, concept, knowledge, registry, cross-session, obsidian]
aliases: [knowledge registry, knowledge vault, cross-session memory, knowledge/]
slug: concepts/knowledge-registry
---

# Knowledge Registry — Durable Memory Across Sessions

> An Obsidian-flavored markdown vault in your project that records what worked, what didn't, and why — and survives every session.

See also: [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[06-state-files|State Files Reference]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[commands/06-update-docs|update-docs]]

---

## Overview

`memory.md` holds **session state** — tasks, checkpoints, budget — and completed sessions are eventually archived. The knowledge registry holds **durable learnings**: decisions made, approaches that succeeded, approaches that failed (and under what conditions they may be retried), binding constraints, and general insights. Registry entries are never archived and never deleted; they persist for the life of the project and bridge every session.

**Rule**: durable learnings live in the registry; session-scoped working data stays in `memory.md`. Neither is duplicated into the other.

All reads and writes go through the operations defined in `lib/knowledge-protocol.md` (OP-1 through OP-9) — commands never manipulate registry files ad hoc.

> [!note] Location is configurable
> The registry lives in your **target project** at the path set by `knowledge_path` in `memory.md` Config (default `knowledge/`). Commit it to share project knowledge with your team, or `.gitignore` it — your choice.

---

## Vault Layout

```
{knowledge_path}/
├── index.md                  # entries grouped by type
├── timeline.md               # reverse-chronological session sections
└── entries/
    └── K-NNN-short-slug.md   # one atomic note per entry
```

- **`index.md`** — every entry, grouped by type, with status. The registry's table of contents.
- **`timeline.md`** — one section per session (newest first): goal, outcome, entries captured, and any reversal. Reads as the project's evolution story.
- **`entries/`** — atomic notes named `K-NNN-short-slug.md`. K-NNN IDs are allocated like Task IDs: highest existing ID + 1, zero-padded 3 digits.

The entry note and its `index.md` row are two views of one record (Registry Dual-Update Rule): every capture or supersession writes both in the same operation.

---

## Entry Schema

Each entry note carries YAML frontmatter:

| Field | Required | Values |
|-------|----------|--------|
| `id` | yes | `K-NNN` |
| `title` | yes | Short, descriptive |
| `type` | yes | `decision` · `success` · `failure` · `constraint` · `insight` |
| `status` | yes | `active` · `superseded` |
| `created` | yes | ISO 8601 timestamp |
| `session` | yes | Session ID (`S-...`) or `manual` |
| `tags` | yes | Always includes `knowledge-registry` |
| `task` | optional | Task ID (`T-NNN`) the entry relates to |
| `supersedes` | optional | Wikilink to the entry this one replaces |
| `superseded_by` | optional | Wikilink to the entry that replaced this one |

The body holds Description, Rationale, and Context sections; `failure` entries add `## Retry Conditions` (conditions under which the approach may be retried — "none known" is valid).

---

## Lifecycle Integration

| Command | Registry interaction |
|---------|---------------------|
| [[commands/01-start\|start]] | Creates the registry if absent (OP-1 — the **only** command allowed to). Selective-load at Section 4a; consult at task decomposition; success capture at each task completion; timeline section at session close. |
| [[commands/02-resume\|resume]] | Selective-load at Section 2c; consult during interrupted-task resolution — recorded failures and retry conditions inform the resume-vs-rollback recommendation. |
| [[commands/03-status\|status]] | Adds a Knowledge stats line to the dashboard. |
| [[commands/04-rollback\|rollback]] | Captures the failure after rollback completes; rewrites a prior success entry in place if one exists for the task. |
| [[commands/05-report\|report]] | Uses registry context for the report and updates the session's timeline section. |
| [[commands/06-update-docs\|update-docs]] | Docs-sync: reads entries newer than `last_knowledge_docs_sync` and weaves their decisions and rationale into the documentation; captures doc-task completions; sets the sync timestamp on success only. |
| Any command | Manual capture: say "remember that…" and the learning is recorded immediately (`type` as stated, default `insight`). |

Capture is **incremental** — an entry must be on disk before the next task begins, so an interrupted session loses at most the in-flight step.

Two Config fields in `memory.md` support the registry:

| Field | Default | Description |
|-------|---------|-------------|
| `knowledge_path` | `knowledge/` | Registry location in the target project |
| `last_knowledge_docs_sync` | `never` | Timestamp of the last successful docs-sync (`never` = all entries are unsynced) |

---

## Supersession Semantics

When a new learning contradicts an active entry, the old note is **never deleted** — contradicted knowledge is history, not garbage:

1. The new entry gets `supersedes: "[[old-slug]]"`.
2. The old entry gets `status: superseded` and `superseded_by: "[[new-slug]]"`.
3. Both `index.md` rows are updated in the same operation.

When loading, the newest `active` entry on any topic wins.

A related case: when a task is rolled back **after** its success was captured, the same note is rewritten in place — `type` flips to `failure`, the `id` and filename are kept, and a callout is appended:

> [!warning] Rolled back
> Rolled back in {sessionId} ({timestamp}): {reason}.

No second entry is created.

---

## Selective Loading

Startup cost stays flat as the registry grows because `start` and `resume` load only:

1. `index.md` in full
2. The 3 most recent session sections of `timeline.md`
3. Active entries that are `failure` or `constraint`, or whose tags match keywords from the stated session goal

Everything else loads lazily when a topic match arises during consultation. After loading:

```
Knowledge registry: {N} entries loaded ({F} failures, {C} constraints, {M} goal-matched) of {T} total.
```

During planning, any proposed approach that overlaps a recorded failure is flagged — never silently adopted:

```
Prior failure on record: [[K-NNN-slug]] — {title} ({session}). Reason: {rationale}. Proceed anyway, adapt, or skip?
```

`constraint` entries are binding context: any constraint an approach would violate is surfaced before the task is finalized.

---

## Tolerance Rules

Registry problems **never abort a session** — worst case is a warning and degraded knowledge context:

- **Unparseable entry** (broken frontmatter, missing required field): warn once per file per session and skip it — `Warning: knowledge entry '{path}' could not be parsed ({reason}). Entry skipped; fix or remove it.`
- **Duplicate `id`**: use the newest by `created` date, with a warning.
- **Missing optional metadata**: accepted silently.
- **Registry absent** (and the command is not `start`): one-line note that `/kandalf:start` will create it; the operation continues as a no-op.
- The harness never deletes a registry file.

---

## Browsing in Obsidian

The registry is a self-contained Obsidian-compatible vault — open `knowledge/` in Obsidian (or any markdown tool) to browse it without the harness:

- `index.md` is the entry point, grouped by type
- `timeline.md` reads as the project's chronological story
- Wikilinks connect entries, supersession chains, and timeline sections; the graph view shows how knowledge evolved
- The `knowledge-registry` tag (on every entry) makes search and filtering trivial

> [!tip] Built in: obsidian-markdown skill
> Notes are written as Obsidian Flavored Markdown. The `obsidian-markdown` skill (vendored from [kepano/obsidian-skills](https://github.com/kepano/obsidian-skills), MIT) ships with the plugin at `kandalf/skills/obsidian-markdown/` — nothing to install. `lib/knowledge-protocol.md` summarizes the same conventions (properties, wikilinks, callouts).

📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/04-budget-detection.md
==============================
---
title: "Budget Detection"
tags: [kandalf, concept, budget, tokens, graceful-shutdown, resilience]
aliases: [budget detection, token budget, graceful shutdown, resilience]
slug: concepts/budget-detection
---

# Budget Detection

> Token budget estimation and graceful shutdown algorithm.

See also: [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]]

---

## Overview

kandalf does not have privileged access to the Claude API token counter. Instead, it maintains a **running estimate** of tokens consumed, using conservative upper-bound per-operation costs. When the estimate crosses the configured threshold, a graceful shutdown is triggered — no work is lost.

---

## Configuration

Budget settings live in `memory.md` Config section:

| Field | Default | How to Override |
|-------|---------|-----------------|
| `budget_limit` | 100000 | `--budget-limit <tokens>` on `/kandalf:start` |
| `budget_threshold_pct` | 80 | `--budget-threshold <pct>` on start or resume |

**Shutdown threshold**: `budget_limit × (budget_threshold_pct / 100)`

Example: `budget_limit=100000`, `budget_threshold_pct=80` → shutdown at 80,000 estimated tokens.

---

## Estimation Algorithm

```
tokens_estimated = 0

On each operation:
  tokens_estimated += upper_bound_estimate(operation)
```

**Operation cost estimates** (conservative upper bounds used):

| Operation | Estimated Tokens |
|-----------|-----------------|
| Read `memory.md` (full) | 2,000–8,000 |
| Write checkpoint JSON | 500–2,000 |
| Sub-agent spawn (overhead) | 1,000 |
| Sub-agent `analysis` task | 5,000–20,000 |
| Sub-agent `change` task (small file) | 3,000–10,000 |
| Sub-agent `change` task (large file) | 10,000–30,000 |
| `codegraph_impact` query | 200–500 |
| `codegraph_context` query | 1,000–5,000 |
| User interaction round-trip | 500–2,000 |
| Session Handoff Summary | 1,000–3,000 |

Upper bounds are always used for conservative estimation.

---

## Threshold Check

Run **before** spawning a sub-agent or starting a new task step:

```
if tokens_estimated >= shutdown_threshold:
    TRIGGER GRACEFUL SHUTDOWN

if tokens_estimated + expected_next_task_cost > shutdown_threshold:
    DO NOT start the next task
    Include it in Session Handoff Summary as "not started"
```

---

## Graceful Shutdown Sequence

> [!note] No work is lost on shutdown
> The current atomic step always completes before stopping. A checkpoint is written and the session can be resumed with `/kandalf:resume`.

When threshold is crossed or approached (9 steps, mandatory order):

1. **Complete the current atomic step** — never abandon mid-write; if a sub-agent is mid-[[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|checkpoint protocol]], let it finish steps 4–6
2. **Write checkpoint** — ensure the current task has a valid checkpoint
3. **Update task status** — set to `interrupted` in Task Registry and Task Details; clear Owner
4. **Write session end** — update Session Log with End Time and Outcome: `interrupted-budget`, `interrupted-quota`, or `interrupted-user`
5. **Update Budget Tracking table** — append row with tokens estimated and outcome
6. **Generate Session Handoff Summary** — from `kandalf/templates/session-handoff-template.md`; fill: tasks completed, tasks interrupted (with last checkpoint and next action), tasks not started, budget status, recommended next session scope
7. **Append Session Handoff Summary to memory.md** — after the Open Questions section; visible to the next session on load
8. **Write session JSON** — `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json` with full execution trace
9. **Output the Session Handoff Summary** — final output before the session ends

---

## Recommended Next Session Scope

Computed during graceful shutdown (step 6):

```
remaining_budget = budget_limit          # fresh session starts with full budget
safety_margin    = budget_limit × 0.15  # reserve 15% for overhead and shutdown
usable           = budget_limit - safety_margin

for each pending task (in dependency order):
    cost = upper_bound_estimate(task.estimated_effort)
    if usable - cost >= 0:
        include in recommendation
        usable -= cost
    else:
        stop
```

**Effort → cost mapping**:
- `small` → 15,000 tokens
- `medium` → 35,000 tokens
- `large` → 70,000 tokens

---

## Budget Tracking Table

One row appended per session:

```markdown
| Session ID | Tokens Estimated | Budget Limit | Interrupted | Resumed By |
|------------|-----------------|--------------|-------------|------------|
| S-20260521-093045 | 42000 | 100000 | no | — |
| S-20260521-112233 | 79500 | 100000 | yes | S-20260521-143000 |
```

`Interrupted` = `yes` for any outcome other than `completed`.
`Resumed By` = filled by the next session that resumes interrupted tasks.

---

## How to Avoid Mid-Session Shutdowns

- Use `--budget-limit` to set a higher limit for large projects with many change tasks
- Use `--budget-threshold 90` if you want more runway before shutdown kicks in
- Start with `analysis` tasks first (cheaper) to understand scope before committing to `change` tasks
- Use `/kandalf:status` to monitor `Budget: N% used` before starting a session

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/05-sub-agent-model.md
==============================
---
title: "Sub-Agent Model"
tags: [kandalf, concept, sub-agent, parallelism, coordination]
aliases: [sub-agent model, sub-agents, parallel execution, agent coordination]
slug: concepts/sub-agent-model
---

# Sub-Agent Model

> How kandalf coordinates parallel and sequential sub-agents within a session.

See also: [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]]

---

## Overview

kandalf runs as the **parent session** in Claude Code. For work that can be parallelized or that benefits from isolated execution, it spawns **sub-agents** — additional Claude agents operating within the same Claude Code session.

Sub-agents are not separate Claude Code processes; they share the same workspace and memory.md state. The parent session coordinates their work through [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md]] as shared state.

---

## When Sub-Agents Are Used

| Scenario | Model |
|----------|-------|
| Small `analysis` task | Executed inline by parent session |
| `change` task | Sub-agent spawned (isolated execution context) |
| Parallelizable tasks (no overlapping output files) | Multiple sub-agents spawned concurrently |
| Single task with complex steps | Multiple coordinated sub-agents for the same task |

---

## Parallelizability Rules

A task is parallelizable if it has no overlapping `Outputs` with any other currently `in_progress` task. The parent session checks this before spawning.

**Example**: T-003 modifies `prompts/system.md` and T-004 modifies `config/tools.json`. These tasks can run in parallel because they own different files.

**Non-example**: T-005 and T-006 both modify `prompts/system.md`. They must run sequentially.

---

## Sub-Agent Instruction Template

When spawning a sub-agent, the parent session includes this prompt:

```
This project has CodeGraph initialized (.codegraph/ exists).
You are executing task {taskId}: {title}.

Mandatory rules:
1. Read kandalf/lib/codegraph-integration.md before modifying any file.
2. Follow the 10-step data flow for change tasks exactly.
3. Follow kandalf/lib/checkpoint-protocol.md for every file write.
4. Log your codegraph_impact result to memory.md task {taskId} CodeGraph Impact field.
5. Do not modify any file listed in another in-progress task's Outputs.

When done, update task {taskId} status to 'completed' in memory.md and write your
session end to the Session Log.
```

---

## File Ownership Protocol

> [!warning] File ownership is mandatory — no exceptions
> A sub-agent that modifies a file owned by another in-progress task causes data corruption. The check must run before every write, not just at spawn time.

Before touching any file, every sub-agent must:

1. Read Task Registry in `memory.md`
2. Check if any `in_progress` task lists that file in its Outputs
3. If yes: **HALT** — file is owned by another task; alert parent session
4. If no: proceed and do not modify files outside its declared `Outputs` list

This prevents concurrent writes to the same file even when sub-agents run in parallel.

---

## Single Task with Multiple Sub-Agents

A single task may need multiple sub-agents working in sequence:

**Example: T-007 "Improve tool call accuracy"**
- Sub-agent A: Analyzes current tool call patterns via `codegraph_context`
- Sub-agent B (after A): Writes the updated tool definitions
- Sub-agent C (after B): Validates the changes and updates task Notes

Each sub-agent writes its findings to the same `### T-007:` subsection in `memory.md`. The parent session coordinates the handoff between sub-agents.

---

## State Coordination via memory.md

All sub-agents coordinate through `memory.md`:

- **Before starting**: read Task Registry to check file ownership
- **When claiming a file**: update task status to `in_progress` with session ID as Owner
- **During execution**: write checkpoint entries, CodeGraph impact logs, Change Log entries
- **On completion**: update task status to `completed`, clear Owner, write to Session Log

The parent session monitors sub-agent completion and advances to the next task when ready.

---

## Budget Impact

Each sub-agent spawn adds ~1,000 tokens to the estimated budget (overhead). The parent session accounts for this in its [[concepts/04-budget-detection|budget estimation]] before spawning.

For a session with 5 tasks all requiring sub-agents: add ~5,000 tokens overhead to the estimated total.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/01-memory-schema.md
==============================
---
title: "memory.md Schema"
tags: [kandalf, concept, memory, state, schema]
aliases: [memory.md, memory schema, session state]
slug: concepts/memory-schema
---

# memory.md — Session State Schema

> The single source of truth for all kandalf session state.

See also: [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]]

---

## Overview

`memory.md` lives at your **project root** (alongside `memory.md`, not inside `kandalf/`). Every command reads it before doing any work. Every change to state — task registration, execution, interruption, rollback — is written to `memory.md` first.

**Rule**: No file in your agent project is touched until the task is registered in `memory.md`.

**Validation**: the session-start checklist (Rules 1–6) is summarized in `lib/common-preamble.md`; full definitions stay in `lib/memory-schema.md`. Registration-time integrity rules (Rule 7 uniqueness, Rule 8 referential completeness, Rule 9 acyclicity) reject invalid task plans atomically before anything is written.

**Bounded growth**: completed sessions beyond the `archive_keep_sessions` most recent (default 1) are moved to per-session records in `.kandalf/archive/`, indexed by the `## Archive Index` section — see `lib/archive-protocol.md`.

---

## Required Sections

All 10 sections must be present. A missing or malformed section triggers the FR-016 repair flow.

| Section | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `## Config` | Budget settings, session counter, creation date |
| `## Project Goal` | One-paragraph summary of the improvement objective |
| `## Scope` | Bullet list of in-scope components |
| `## Budget & Quota Tracking` | Per-session budget usage table |
| `## Task Registry` | Table of all tasks and their current status |
| `## Task Details` | One subsection per task with full metadata |
| `## Change Log` | Record of every file modification (planned → applied → rolled-back) |
| `## Session Log` | One row per session: start time, end time, outcome |
| `## Checkpoint Registry` | References to all checkpoint JSON files |
| `## Open Questions` | Unresolved items from discovery Q&A |

---

## Config Fields

```markdown
## Config

- budget_limit: 100000
- budget_threshold_pct: 80
- session_count: 1
- created: 2026-05-21
- codegraph_baseline_symbols: 847
- codegraph_baseline_sync: 2026-05-21T09:30:45Z
- codegraph_backend: native
```

| Field | Default | Description |
|-------|---------|-------------|
| `budget_limit` | 100000 | Estimated total token budget |
| `budget_threshold_pct` | 80 | Graceful shutdown threshold (1–99) |
| `session_count` | 0 | Increments on each session start |
| `created` | (set at init) | Date memory.md was first created (YYYY-MM-DD) |
| `codegraph_baseline_symbols` | (from init) | Symbol count at session start |
| `codegraph_baseline_sync` | (from init) | ISO timestamp of first CodeGraph sync |
| `codegraph_backend` | (from init) | `native` or `wasm` |

---

## Task Registry

Markdown table with one row per task:

```markdown
| Task ID | Title | Type | Status | Owner | Depends On | Session | Checkpoint |
|---------|-------|------|--------|-------|------------|---------|------------|
| T-001 | Analyze system prompt | analysis | completed | — | none | S-20260521-093045 | none |
| T-002 | Rewrite tone section | change | in_progress | S-20260521-093045 | T-001 | S-20260521-093045 | T-002-step2-S-20260521-093045 |
```

### Task ID Format

`T-NNN` — zero-padded 3 digits (e.g., `T-001`). Extended to `T-NNNN` if >999 tasks.

### Status Enum

| Status | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `pending` | Not yet started |
| `in_progress` | Actively executing (locked by session or sub-agent) |
| `interrupted` | Was in_progress when budget/quota was exhausted; safe to resume |
| `blocked` | Waiting on another task or external input |
| `completed` | Done and verified against success criteria |
| `deferred` | Intentionally postponed |

### Status Transitions

```
pending     → in_progress   (session claims task)
in_progress → completed     (verified against success criteria)
in_progress → interrupted   (budget/quota exhaustion or session end)
interrupted → in_progress   (resumed from checkpoint)
interrupted → pending       (rolled back)
pending     → blocked       (dependency incomplete)
blocked     → pending       (dependency completed)
pending     → deferred      (user decision)
```

**Prohibited**: `completed` → any status (terminal; only rollback can reset to `pending`)
**Prohibited**: `in_progress` with empty Owner

---

## Task Details

One `### T-NNN:` subsection per task. Must exist for every row in Task Registry.

```markdown
### T-002: Rewrite tone section

- Type: change
- Status: in_progress
- Owner: S-20260521-093045
- Depends On: T-001
- Assigned Session: S-20260521-093045
- Description: Rewrite the tone guidance section in prompts/system.md to use plain, friendly language
- Inputs: prompts/system.md
- Outputs: prompts/system.md
- Success Criteria: Tone section uses second-person, active voice; no formal titles like "Greetings"
- Estimated Effort: small
- Parallelizable: yes
- CodeGraph Impact: [2026-05-21T09:44:00Z] codegraph_impact("systemPrompt"): 4 symbols across 2 files. Affected: systemPrompt (prompts/system.md), toneConfig (config/agent.json). Test files: tests/tone.test.js
- Checkpoint: T-002-step2-S-20260521-093045
- Resume Instructions: Continue from step 3 — write new content to .kandalf/tmp/prompts__system.md.tmp and rename to target
- Notes: —
```

---

## Change Log

```markdown
| Timestamp | Task ID | File | Change Summary | Status | Rationale |
|-----------|---------|------|----------------|--------|-----------|
| 2026-05-21T09:44:00Z | T-002 | prompts/system.md | Rewrite tone guidance | planned | Discovery identified overly formal language |
| 2026-05-21T09:44:35Z | T-002 | prompts/system.md | Rewrite tone guidance | applied | Discovery identified overly formal language |
```

Status values: `planned` → `applied` → `rolled-back`

A `planned` entry with no corresponding `applied` entry is an incomplete operation — detectable by `/kandalf:resume`.

---

## Session Log

```markdown
| Session ID | Start Time | End Time | Tasks Claimed | Outcome |
|------------|------------|----------|---------------|---------|
| S-20260521-093045 | 2026-05-21T09:30:45Z | — | T-001, T-002 | active |
```

Outcome values: `completed` · `interrupted-budget` · `interrupted-quota` · `interrupted-user` · `error` · `active`

---

## Checkpoint Registry

```markdown
| Checkpoint ID | Task ID | Step | Session ID | Files Snapshotted |
|---------------|---------|------|------------|-------------------|
| T-002-step1-S-20260521-093045 | T-002 | 1 | S-20260521-093045 | prompts/system.md |
| T-002-step2-S-20260521-093045 | T-002 | 2 | S-20260521-093045 | prompts/system.md |
```

Each checkpoint corresponds to a JSON file at `.kandalf/checkpoints/{checkpointId}.json`. See [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]].

---

## Validation Rules

kandalf validates all 5 rules on every session start. Violation triggers the FR-016 repair flow.

| Rule | Check |
|------|-------|
| Rule 1 | Every Task Registry row has a matching `### T-NNN:` in Task Details |
| Rule 2 | No task has status `in_progress` with empty Owner |
| Rule 3 | Every Owner value matches a Session ID in the Session Log |
| Rule 4 | Every non-`none` Checkpoint matches a row in Checkpoint Registry |
| Rule 5 | `budget_threshold_pct` is an integer between 1 and 99 |

---

## ID Formats

| ID Type | Format | Example |
|---------|--------|---------|
| Session ID | `S-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}` | `S-20260521-093045` |
| Task ID | `T-{NNN}` | `T-001` |
| Checkpoint ID | `{taskId}-step{N}-{sessionId}` | `T-001-step2-S-20260521-093045` |

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/03-codegraph-integration.md
==============================
---
title: "CodeGraph Integration"
tags: [kandalf, concept, codegraph, impact-analysis, semantic-graph]
aliases: [CodeGraph, codegraph integration, impact analysis]
slug: concepts/codegraph-integration
---

# CodeGraph Integration

> Mandatory semantic impact analysis before every file modification.

See also: [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## What Is CodeGraph?

CodeGraph (`@colbymchenry/codegraph`) is an MCP server that builds a semantic graph of your codebase. It understands the call graph, imports, symbol references, and file relationships — not just text patterns.

kandalf uses CodeGraph to:
- Find the exact symbol or file to modify (not just text-search)
- Measure the blast radius of a proposed change before touching anything
- Identify which test files need re-running after a change
- Generate quantitative data for the improvement report

---

## Setup

The CodeGraph MCP server **ships with the plugin** — `kandalf/.mcp.json` starts it automatically (`npx -y @colbymchenry/codegraph serve --mcp`) when the plugin loads. No install, no Claude Code restart.

Only the project index needs to exist. `/kandalf:start` builds it automatically when `.codegraph/` is missing; to do it manually, run from your project root (**outside** the kandalf directory):

```bash
cd /path/to/your-agent-project
codegraph init -i        # or: npx -y @colbymchenry/codegraph init -i
```

---

## Available MCP Tools

| Tool | Purpose | When Used |
|------|---------|-----------|
| `codegraph_search` | Find symbols by name across the codebase | First step when locating a target function, class, or file |
| `codegraph_context` | Build relevant code context for a task | During `analysis` tasks to understand a component's role |
| `codegraph_impact` | Analyze what code is affected by changing a symbol | **Mandatory** before any file write in a `change` task |
| `codegraph_callers` | Find what calls a function | Understanding upstream dependencies before refactoring |
| `codegraph_callees` | Find what a function calls | Understanding a function's reach before changing it |
| `codegraph_node` | Get details about a specific symbol | Inspecting a single symbol's definition and source |
| `codegraph_files` | Get indexed file structure | Faster than filesystem scanning for project layout |
| `codegraph_status` | Check index health and statistics | Session start baseline + report generation |
| `codegraph_affected` | Find test files affected by changed source files | After every file write to identify tests to run |

---

## The 10-Step Data Flow for Change Tasks

Every `change` task sub-agent **must** execute these steps in order:

```
1. codegraph_search("{target symbol or filename}")
   Locate the exact symbol or file. Confirm it is the intended target.

2. codegraph_impact("{symbol}", depth=3)
   Blast-radius analysis. If impact > 20 symbols: HALT and alert parent session.

3. Log impact to memory.md Task Details (CodeGraph Impact field)
   Required BEFORE writing rollback copy or checkpoint.

4. codegraph_callers("{symbol}")
   Understand all upstream dependencies.

5. Write rollback copy → .kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/   [Checkpoint Step 1]

6. Append planned entry to Change Log                         [Checkpoint Step 2]

7. Write checkpoint JSON                                       [Checkpoint Step 3]

8. Write new content to .kandalf/tmp/{file}.tmp         [Checkpoint Step 4]

9. Rename tmp → target path                                    [Checkpoint Step 5]

10. codegraph_affected([modified files])
    Identify test files. Log them in task Notes. Update Change Log to applied.  [Checkpoint Step 6]
```

---

## Impact Radius Thresholds

> [!warning] 21+ symbols = hard stop
> A sub-agent must not proceed unilaterally when impact exceeds 20 symbols. Always surface this to the parent session for task splitting or explicit confirmation.

| Impact Radius | Action |
|---------------|--------|
| 0–5 symbols | Proceed normally |
| 6–20 symbols | Log all affected symbols; proceed |
| 21+ symbols | **HALT** — alert parent session; request task split or explicit user confirmation |
| Cross-component boundary | Note the boundary being crossed; proceed only after confirmation |

---

## Impact Log Format

After every `codegraph_impact` call, the sub-agent logs to the task's `CodeGraph Impact` field in `memory.md`:

```
[2026-05-21T09:44:00Z] codegraph_impact("systemPrompt"): 4 symbols across 2 files.
Affected: systemPrompt (prompts/system.md), toneConfig (config/agent.json).
Test files to run: tests/tone.test.js
```

This log feeds the **Impact radius** line in the `/kandalf:report` output.

---

## Rules for Analysis Tasks

`analysis` tasks **do not** modify files. They use:
- `codegraph_context` and `codegraph_search` for exploration (not grep/filesystem scan)
- `codegraph_node` to inspect specific symbols
- Results written to the task's Notes field in `memory.md`
- Do **not** call `codegraph_impact` unless analyzing impact for a planned future change

---

## File Ownership Rules

Before a sub-agent touches any file, it must:

1. Read the Task Registry in `memory.md`
2. Check if any `in_progress` task lists that file in its Outputs
3. If yes: **HALT** — another task owns this file; alert parent session
4. If no: proceed

A sub-agent must not modify any file outside its task's declared `Outputs` list.

---

## Auto-Sync Behavior

CodeGraph auto-syncs on file save (2-second debounce). After any batch of file changes:

1. Wait ~3 seconds for auto-sync
2. Run `codegraph status` to confirm index is fresh
3. If still stale: run `codegraph sync` manually

After rollback operations, **always** run `codegraph sync` explicitly.

---

## Prerequisite Check

Every sub-agent must verify `.codegraph/` exists in the project root before doing any work. If absent: alert the parent session — the user must run `codegraph install --yes` from the project root (outside the kandalf directory). No files are modified until the index is built.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/concepts/07-compression-policy.md
==============================
---
title: "Compression Policy (caveman)"
tags: [kandalf, concept, compression, caveman, tokens]
aliases: [compression policy, caveman policy, output compression]
slug: compression-policy
---

# Compression Policy (caveman)

> How Kandalf compresses its conversational output dynamically — heavy where words don't matter, light where nuance does, off where stakeholders read.

See also: [[05-sub-agent-model|Sub-Agent Model]] · [[01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[00-index|Index]]

Authoritative protocol: `lib/compression-policy.md`. This page is the concept overview; the lib file wins on any conflict.

---

## What it is

Kandalf ships the [caveman](https://github.com/JuliusBrussee/caveman) output-compression ruleset **built in** (vendored at `skills/caveman/SKILL.md`, MIT — nothing to install) and applies it **dynamically by work category**:

| Category | Examples | Compression | Sub-agent model tier |
|---|---|---|---|
| simple | `/kandalf:status`, `/kandalf:rollback`, small analysis/validation tasks | caveman `full` | standard (`sonnet`) |
| complex | `change` tasks, medium/large efforts, failure diagnosis | caveman `lite` | premium (`opus`) |
| planning / artifacts | discovery Q&A, plan confirmation, reports, everything written to disk | off — normal prose | session model |

`ultra` and `wenyan` are never applied automatically.

## Session state

One Config field in `memory.md`: `compression_enabled: true|false`.

- **Default-on**: every session starts compressed; absent field means `true` (older memory.md files keep working).
- **Opt-out, one step**: `--no-compression` on `/kandalf:start`, or reply `no compression` to the session-start notice.
- **Mid-session toggle**: say `normal mode` / `caveman mode` — effective next response, Config written the same turn, so `/kandalf:resume` always restores the latest state.
- **Nothing to install**: the ruleset is part of the plugin; there is no detection step and no degraded mode — the Config switch is the only state.

## Invariants

- Code, file paths, identifiers, commands, URLs, numbers: **byte-preserved at every level**.
- Tables, checklists, exact-output strings (contract-defined errors, notices): never reworded.
- **Persisted artifacts are always full prose** — reports, memory.md, knowledge entries, docs. Compression is for the conversation, not the record.
- Mixed-category responses use the lightest involved compression.

## Why

Caveman's published benchmarks average ~65% output-token reduction at full technical accuracy; Kandalf targets ≥40% per session with zero accuracy loss (spec 016 SC-001). Less reading for you, longer sessions per budget.
