# /kandalf:resume

> Resume an interrupted or paused improvement session.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/03-status|status]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:resume
  --budget-threshold <pct>   Optional. Override shutdown threshold for this session only.
```

---

## What It Does

`/kandalf:resume` continues an existing session from its last saved state:

1. **Verifies memory.md** — must exist at project root; runs all 5 validation rules
2. **Detects stale locks** — tasks with status `in_progress` owned by a previous session ID are treated as interrupted
3. **Finds interrupted tasks** — collects tasks with status `interrupted` or stale `in_progress`
4. **Presents interrupted tasks** — for each, shows: last checkpoint, last step completed, and three options
5. **Resolves interrupted tasks** — per your choice: resume from checkpoint, roll back, or defer
6. **Presents pending tasks** — shows all remaining `pending` tasks; you confirm which to execute
7. **Executes selected tasks** — same loop as `/kandalf:start` Section 8: sub-agents, checkpoint protocol, CodeGraph, budget check
8. **Closes session** — same as `/kandalf:start` Section 10

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> After `memory.md` validation (Section 2c), `resume` selectively loads [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] entries — failures and constraints always, plus goal-matched entries. During interrupted-task resolution, recorded failures and their retry conditions inform the resume-vs-rollback recommendation (the entry is cited when it changes the advice). If the registry is absent, resume notes it and continues — only `start` creates it.

---

## Parameters

### `--budget-threshold <pct>` *(optional)*

Override the shutdown threshold for this resume session. Useful if you want to run a longer or shorter session than the default stored in `memory.md`.

---

## Interrupted Task Options

For each interrupted task, you are shown:

```
Interrupted task: T-003 — Rewrite system prompt tone
  Status: interrupted
  Last checkpoint: T-003-step2-S-20260521-093045
  Last step: 2
  Next action: Continue from step 3 — write new content to tmp/ and rename to target
  Options: (a) Resume from checkpoint  (b) Roll back to pre-task state  (c) Skip (defer)
```

### (a) Resume from checkpoint

Restores file state from the checkpoint JSON. Continues execution from the step after the last completed step. Does not re-do any steps already done.

### (b) Roll back to pre-task state

Reads the rollback copy from `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/`. Restores each file to its pre-task original. Sets task status back to `pending`. Runs `codegraph sync`.

Use this if the partial change is incorrect and you want to retry cleanly.

### (c) Skip (defer)

Sets task status to `deferred`. No file changes. The task remains in Task Registry and can be resumed in a future session.

---

## Prerequisites

- `memory.md` must exist at the project root (created by `/kandalf:start`)
- At least one task must have status `interrupted` or `pending`; if all tasks are already `completed`, the session is done

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to begin a new session.` | No active session exists | Run `/kandalf:start --docs <path>` to begin |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted state file | Follow repair options: reset to checkpoint or archive + start fresh |

---

## Examples

```
# Resume after Claude Code was closed mid-session
/kandalf:resume

# Resume with a tighter budget threshold for a quick catch-up session
/kandalf:resume --budget-threshold 60
```

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/commands/04-rollback.md
==============================
---
title: "/kandalf:rollback"
tags: [kandalf, command, rollback, recovery]
aliases: [rollback command, kandalf rollback, undo]
slug: commands/rollback
---

# /kandalf:rollback

> Roll back task changes to restore files to their pre-task state.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|Checkpoint Protocol]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## Syntax — Three Forms

```
/kandalf:rollback T-NNN
    Roll back a specific completed task.

/kandalf:rollback --all
    Roll back all completed tasks (in reverse Task ID order).

/kandalf:rollback --to S-YYYYMMDD-HHmmss
    Roll back all tasks completed after a specific session boundary.
```

---

## What It Does

For each targeted task, rollback:

1. Validates `memory.md` and the task's preconditions
2. Shows a **preview** of files to be restored (requires your confirmation before any change)
3. Reads the original file copies from `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/`
4. Writes current files to `.kandalf/tmp/` as a safety copy
5. Atomically renames rollback files → target paths
6. Updates `memory.md`: task status → `pending`, Owner cleared, Checkpoint cleared
7. Appends `rolled-back` entries to Change Log
8. Runs `codegraph sync` to rebuild the index

---

## Form 1: Per-Task Rollback (`T-NNN`)

Rolls back a single task. The task must have status `completed` and rollback files must exist in `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-NNN/`.

```
/kandalf:rollback T-003
```

**Preview shown before applying**:
```
Rollback preview for T-003 (Rewrite system prompt tone section):
  Files to restore:
    - prompts/system.md (last modified: 2026-05-21T09:45:12Z)

Proceed? (yes/no)
```

After rollback: T-003 status resets to `pending`. You can re-execute it with `/kandalf:resume`.

---

## Form 2: Full Rollback (`--all`)

Rolls back all tasks with status `completed`, in **reverse Task ID order** (highest T-NNN first). This ensures dependent tasks are undone before the tasks they depended on.

```
/kandalf:rollback --all
```

If more than 3 tasks are targeted, you confirm each task's rollback individually before it is applied.

After rollback: All tasks reset to `pending`. `memory.md` Change Log shows all files as `rolled-back`.

---

## Form 3: Session-Boundary Rollback (`--to S-XXX`)

Rolls back all tasks whose completion timestamp (from the Change Log) is **after** session `S-XXX`'s end time. Useful to undo everything from a specific session without touching earlier work.

```
/kandalf:rollback --to S-20260521-093045
```

Use `/kandalf:status` to see session IDs, or check the Session Log in `memory.md`.

---

## Preconditions

| Form | Required |
|------|---------|
| `T-NNN` | Task exists in Task Registry; status = `completed`; `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-NNN/` exists |
| `--all` | At least one task with status `completed` |
| `--to S-XXX` | Session `S-XXX` exists in Session Log |

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: task T-NNN not found in Task Registry.` | Task ID doesn't exist | Check `/kandalf:status` for valid IDs |
| `Error: T-NNN has status 'pending' — only completed tasks can be rolled back.` | Task was never completed | Nothing to roll back; task is already in original state |
| `Error: rollback files for T-NNN not found in .kandalf/rollbacks/.` | Rollback directory missing | Manually review the Change Log in `memory.md` for original content |
| `Error: session S-XXX not found in Session Log.` | Invalid session ID | Run `/kandalf:status` to see valid session IDs |

---

## After Rollback

- Tasks reset to `pending` — re-execute with `/kandalf:resume`
- `codegraph sync` is run automatically to keep the index consistent
- The original rollback copies in `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` are preserved (not deleted) — the first copy is always the true pre-kandalf original

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> After rollback completes, the failure is captured in the [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]]: a `failure` entry with retry conditions is created, or — if a success entry was already captured for the task — that same note is rewritten in place (`type` flipped to `failure`, a `> [!warning] Rolled back` callout appended; no second entry). A reversal line is added to the session's `timeline.md` section.

---

## Rollback File Encoding

Rollback files use a path-encoding scheme: `/` → `__` and `:` → `__colon__`.

Example: `prompts/system.md` → `.kandalf/rollbacks/T-003/prompts__system.md`

This allows any file path to be stored flat in the rollback directory.

---
title: "/kandalf:update-docs"
tags: [kandalf, command, update-docs, documentation]
aliases: [update-docs command, kandalf update-docs]
slug: commands/update-docs
---

# /kandalf:update-docs

> Keep project documentation in sync with the current codebase using CodeGraph structural analysis.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/04-rollback|rollback]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:update-docs
  --docs <path>          Optional. Documentation directory to scan. Defaults to docs_path in memory.md.
  --enrich               Optional flag. Enrich sparse or empty sections with CodeGraph-derived content.
  --dry-run              Optional flag. Show planned changes without writing any files.
```

---

## What It Does

`/kandalf:update-docs` analyzes your project's documentation against the current codebase and updates sections that have drifted out of sync:

1. **Validates session state** — checks `memory.md` is valid; resolves the docs directory
2. **Verifies CodeGraph** — confirms the index exists; captures baseline metrics
3. **Discovers docs** — scans the docs directory recursively for `.md` files; parses each file into sections
4. **Delta analysis** — uses `git log` (since `last_docs_update`) and CodeGraph symbol lookup to find which sections reference changed code; falls back to full scan on first run
5. **Builds change candidates** — for each outdated section, drafts updated content from CodeGraph; for stale references (deleted symbols), flags the section for manual review
6. **Confirms with you** — shows a planned-changes table; waits for your approval; `--dry-run` exits here
7. **Applies updates** — follows the full [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|6-step checkpoint protocol]] for every doc file write; uses the [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|10-step CodeGraph data flow]] before each write; rollback is always available
8. **Closes session** — records `last_docs_update` in memory.md; outputs a summary of what changed

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> At run start, docs-sync reads [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] entries created since `last_knowledge_docs_sync` (memory.md Config; `never` means all) and weaves their decisions, rationale, and evolution notes into the documentation updates — knowledge that exists nowhere in the codebase. Completed doc tasks capture success entries. The sync timestamp is set **only on successful completion**; an interrupted run re-reads the same window next time.

---

## Parameters

### `--docs <path>` *(optional)*

Override the documentation directory for this run. Accepts absolute or relative paths.

If omitted, the command uses `docs_path` from `memory.md` Config — the value set by `/kandalf:start --docs <path>`.

```
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./agent/docs
```

### `--enrich` *(optional flag)*

Enable enrichment mode. Sections with 0 non-empty lines (empty) or 1–3 non-empty lines (stub) are identified as enrichment candidates. For each candidate, `codegraph_context` is queried with the section heading to generate descriptive content.

All enrichment-generated content is wrapped in `<!-- kpw:enriched -->` / `<!-- /kpw:enriched -->` HTML comments so you can easily identify, review, or strip AI-generated sections later.

Without `--enrich`, stub and empty sections are left unchanged.

```
/kandalf:update-docs --enrich
```

### `--dry-run` *(optional flag)*

Runs all analysis phases and builds the planned-changes table, but writes nothing. Useful for previewing what would change before committing.

```
/kandalf:update-docs --dry-run
/kandalf:update-docs --enrich --dry-run
```

---

## Prerequisites

- `memory.md` must exist (run `/kandalf:start` if it doesn't)
- A docs directory must be resolvable (via `--docs` flag or `docs_path` in memory.md)
- CodeGraph is strongly recommended (run `codegraph install --yes` from your project root if not yet initialized); the command degrades gracefully if absent with a warning

---

## Session Output

### When changes are found and applied

```
Documentation update complete.
  Files scanned: 12
  Sections reviewed: 47
  Sections updated: 8 (standard updates)
  Sections enriched: 3
  Sections flagged for review: 1
  Files modified: docs/api.md, docs/concepts/auth.md, docs/getting-started.md

Run /kandalf:status to see the full task log.
Run /kandalf:rollback to undo changes.
```

### When documentation is already up to date

```
Documentation is already up to date.
  Files scanned: 12
  Sections reviewed: 47
  No updates required.
```

### After `--dry-run`

```
Dry run — no files modified.
Run without --dry-run to apply.
```

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to initialize.` | No active session | Run `/kandalf:start --docs <path>` first |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted session state | Follow offered repair options |
| `Error: docs path not found. Provide --docs <path> or run /kandalf:start first.` | No `docs_path` in memory.md and no `--docs` flag | Pass `--docs` or start a session first |
| `Error: documentation directory not found at '<path>'.` | `--docs` path does not exist | Verify path; use absolute path if needed |
| `Warning: No documentation files found at '<path>'. Nothing to update.` | No `.md` files in docs dir | Check path or add documentation files |
| `Warning: CodeGraph index not found.` | `.codegraph/` absent | Run `codegraph install --yes` from project root; or proceed with limited analysis |

---

## Examples

```
# Standard update — sync docs with recent code changes
/kandalf:update-docs

# Enrich a project with poor documentation
/kandalf:update-docs --enrich

# Preview changes before applying
/kandalf:update-docs --dry-run

# Point to a specific docs directory this run only
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./my-agent/docs

# Full options — preview enrichment on a specific directory
/kandalf:update-docs --docs ./my-agent/docs --enrich --dry-run
```

---

## State Created

After a successful run, the following changes are made:

```
memory.md                              # last_docs_update, last_docs_update_session, and
                                       # last_knowledge_docs_sync set in Config
                                       # Task Registry has N new completed tasks for this run
                                       # Change Log has entries for every section modified

.kandalf/
  checkpoints/{taskId}-step*.json      # Checkpoint per doc file updated (rollback metadata)
  rollbacks/{taskId}/                  # Original doc file copies (pre-update backup)
  sessions/{sessionId}.json            # Full execution trace
```

All modified documentation files can be reverted with `/kandalf:rollback`.

---

## How Delta Detection Works

On the first run (`last_docs_update = null`), the command does a full CodeGraph scan of all sections.

On subsequent runs, it runs:
```
git log --name-only --pretty=format: --since="<last_docs_update>"
```

…to get only the files changed since the last update. CodeGraph then resolves the symbols from those files and maps them to doc sections via `symbols_referenced`. Only the sections that reference changed symbols are updated — preserving carefully-written documentation that remains accurate.

---

## Enrichment Markers

Enriched content is always wrapped in HTML comments you can grep for:

```markdown
<!-- kpw:enriched -->
This section describes the `AuthService` module, which handles...
<!-- /kpw:enriched -->
```

To strip all enriched content: search for `<!-- kpw:enriched -->` blocks and remove them. To keep it: just delete the comment markers.

📂 FILE: ./docs/commands/07-iterate.md

---
title: "/kandalf:iterate"
tags: [kandalf, command, iterate, requirements, session]
aliases: [iterate command, kandalf iterate]
slug: commands/iterate
---

# /kandalf:iterate

> Refine requirements and register new tasks on top of an existing session — mid-session or post-session.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[commands/04-rollback|rollback]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:iterate
  --scope <area>             Optional. Constrain conversation to tasks/requirements
                             matching <area> (keyword match on task title/description).
  --budget-threshold <pct>   Optional. Override shutdown threshold for this session.
                             Valid range: 1–99. Default: value from memory.md Config.
```

---

## What It Does

`/kandalf:iterate` opens a structured requirement-refinement conversation on top of an existing session:

1. **Validates memory.md** — same 5 rules as `/kandalf:start`. Halts with repair options on failure.
2. **Checks for in-progress tasks** — if any tasks have status `in_progress`, warns you and requires explicit confirmation before continuing.
3. **Checks for a saved iterate conversation** — if `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}-iterate.json` exists from a previous interrupted iterate session, asks: *"Resume previous iterate conversation? (yes/no)"*
4. **Displays opening summary**:
   - Current Project Goal
   - Current Scope bullets
   - Task status counts: Completed N / Pending N / Deferred N (filtered to `--scope` area if provided)
5. **Opens refinement conversation** — multi-turn dialogue where you can:
   - Add new requirements
   - Remove or narrow existing requirements
   - Modify scope
   - Ask questions about the current state
6. **Detects conflicts** — if any proposed requirement change conflicts with a completed task, surfaces the conflict inline with three options before continuing (see Conflict Resolution below).
7. **Proposes a Requirement Delta** — presents the full set of changes for confirmation:
   - Updated Project Goal (or "unchanged")
   - Scope additions and removals
   - New tasks to register (with types and dependencies)
   - Pending tasks to defer (with reasons)
   - Unresolved conflicts (if any)
8. **Waits for confirmation** — `yes` / `no` / `revise`:
   - `yes` → writes all changes to `memory.md` and exits
   - `no` → exits without modifying `memory.md`
   - `revise` → returns to the conversation
9. **Writes to memory.md** (on `yes` only):
   - Updates `## Project Goal` with the agreed new text
   - Updates `## Scope` bullets
   - Appends new tasks to `## Task Registry` and `## Task Details` (IDs continue from highest existing T-NNN)
   - Marks superseded pending tasks `deferred` (never deleted); adds reason to their Notes field
   - Adds a Session Log entry with outcome `iterate`
10. **No-change detection** — if the conversation concludes with no requirement changes, exits cleanly without modifying `memory.md` or creating a Session Log entry.
11. **Exits with a summary** — reports goal/scope changes, new tasks added, tasks deferred, and next steps.

---

## Parameters

### `--scope <area>` *(optional)*

Constrains the iterate conversation to tasks and requirements whose Title or Description contains `<area>` (case-insensitive keyword/substring match). Use this when you want to refine a specific part of the project without revisiting the full scope.

```
/kandalf:iterate --scope "tool definitions"
/kandalf:iterate --scope "system prompt"
```

When `--scope` is set:
- The opening task summary shows only matching tasks
- The conversation stays focused on the specified area
- New tasks generated are tagged with the scope area in their Description

### `--budget-threshold <pct>` *(optional)*

Override the shutdown threshold for this iterate session. Valid range: 1–99. Default: the `budget_threshold_pct` value stored in `memory.md` Config.

```
/kandalf:iterate --budget-threshold 60
```

---

## Conversation Protocol

### Opening Message

```
Iterate session S-{ID} started.

Current project goal:
  {## Project Goal content}

Scope:
  {## Scope bullets}

Task summary:
  Completed : {N} tasks
  Pending   : {N} tasks
  Deferred  : {N} tasks
{if --scope: "  Showing only tasks matching '{area}'" }

What would you like to change or add to the requirements?
```

### Conversation Loop

The iterate session is a free-form multi-turn dialogue. During the conversation:
- Ask clarifying questions as needed to fully understand the requirement change
- Refer to specific existing tasks by ID (e.g., "T-003 covers this area") to anchor the discussion
- Build a complete picture of the Requirement Delta before proposing it

### Confirmation Step

Before writing anything to `memory.md`, present the full proposed Requirement Delta:

```
Proposed changes:
  Goal      : [updated text | "unchanged"]
  Scope     : [N additions, N removals | "unchanged"]
  New tasks : [T-{N+1} — {title}, ... | "none"]
  Deferred  : [T-{X} — {title} (reason: {requirement removed}), ... | "none"]
  Conflicts : [list or "none"]

Apply these changes? (yes / no / revise)
```

- `yes` → write to `memory.md`, exit with summary
- `no` → exit without writing (no-op; no Session Log entry created)
- `revise` → continue the conversation

---

## Conflict Resolution

When a proposed requirement conflicts with a completed task, surface the conflict **before** the confirmation step:

```
⚠️  Conflict detected

  New requirement : "{new requirement text}"
  Conflicts with  : T-{NNN} — {title} (status: completed)
  Change Log      : {ISO timestamp} — {change summary}

Options:
  (a) Proceed — register new requirement alongside completed work
                 (completed task unchanged; divergence is intentional)
  (b) Exit and rollback — exit iterate now, run /kandalf:rollback T-{NNN},
                          then re-run /kandalf:iterate
  (c) Modify requirement — continue conversation and revise the new requirement

Your choice:
```

**Hard rule**: Iterate never modifies, deletes, or changes the status of a `completed` task regardless of the option chosen. Option (b) exits iterate and leaves rollback to `/kandalf:rollback`.

---

## Mid-Session Use

When tasks have mixed statuses (some `completed`, some `pending`, some `interrupted`):

- The opening summary shows counts for all statuses
- Pending task re-evaluation happens during the conversation: tasks whose requirement is removed or narrowed are proposed for deferral
- The Requirement Delta confirmation shows which pending tasks will be deferred and why
- `completed` tasks are always listed as context but never changed

### Pending Task Deferral

When a requirement change makes a pending task irrelevant:
- Task status changes from `pending` → `deferred`
- Notes field updated: `Deferred by iterate session S-{ID}: {requirement removed}`
- Task is never deleted from the registry

### Locked Task Warning

If any task has status `in_progress` when iterate is invoked:

```
Warning: {N} task(s) currently in_progress (owner: {Session ID}).
Proceeding may conflict with active execution.

Tasks: T-{NNN} — {title}, ...

Continue anyway? (yes/no)
```

Answering `no` exits iterate. Answering `yes` proceeds with the iterate conversation (the locked tasks are shown in the summary but treated as immutable).

---

## Interruption & Resume

### On Interruption (budget exhaustion or user abort before `yes`)

1. Save conversation state to `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}-iterate.json` (see data model in `specs/012-iterate-command/data-model.md`)
2. Write Session Log entry with outcome `iterate-interrupted`
3. **Do not modify `memory.md`** — no partial writes
4. Output the iterate handoff using `templates/iterate-handoff-template.md`

```
Iterate session interrupted. Progress saved.
To resume: /kandalf:iterate
kandalf will detect the saved conversation and offer to continue.
```

### On Next Invocation (detecting saved state)

If `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}-iterate.json` exists:

```
Found a saved iterate conversation from {timestamp}.
Conversation summary: {SUMMARY_OF_REQUIREMENTS_DISCUSSED}

Resume previous iterate conversation? (yes/no)
```

- `yes` → restore conversation context from the JSON and continue from where it left off
- `no` → discard the saved state and start a fresh iterate conversation

On successful completion after resuming, delete the `-iterate.json` file.

---

## Prerequisites

- Plugin loaded: `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf`
- `memory.md` must exist at the project root (created by `/kandalf:start`)
- At least the `## Project Goal`, `## Scope`, `## Task Registry`, and `## Session Log` sections must be present and valid

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to begin a new session.` | No active session | Run `/kandalf:start --docs <path>` |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted state | Repair options: reset to checkpoint or archive + start fresh |
| `Warning: {N} task(s) currently in_progress. Proceeding may conflict with active execution.` | Locked tasks exist | Confirm with `yes` or exit and wait for execution to complete |
| `Error: --budget-threshold must be an integer between 1 and 99.` | Invalid threshold | Use a value like `80` |

---

## Examples

```
# Post-session — add new requirements after all tasks are complete
/kandalf:iterate

# Mid-session — refine requirements while some tasks are still pending
/kandalf:iterate

# Scoped — only refine the tool definitions area
/kandalf:iterate --scope "tool definitions"

# With custom budget threshold
/kandalf:iterate --budget-threshold 60
```

---

## State Created / Modified

On successful `yes` confirmation, the following sections of `memory.md` are updated:

| Section | Change |
|---------|--------|
| `## Project Goal` | Replaced with agreed new goal text (or unchanged) |
| `## Scope` | Bullets updated (additions appended, removals removed) |
| `## Task Registry` | New `pending` task rows appended; superseded tasks set to `deferred` |
| `## Task Details` | New task subsections appended; deferred tasks' Notes updated |
| `## Session Log` | New row added with outcome `iterate`, start/end time, tasks registered |

**Never modified by iterate**:
- Any task with status `completed`
- `## Budget & Quota Tracking`
- `## Change Log`
- `## Checkpoint Registry`
- `.kandalf/rollbacks/` or `.kandalf/checkpoints/`

On interruption, only `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}-iterate.json` is written. `memory.md` is not touched.

---

## Exit Messages

### Successful completion with changes

```
Iterate session S-{ID} complete.
  Goal updated      : yes / no
  Scope changes     : {N} additions, {N} removals
  New tasks added   : {N} (T-{X} through T-{Y})
  Tasks deferred    : {N}
  Conflicts resolved: {N}

Run /kandalf:execute to execute the new tasks.
Run /kandalf:status to review the full task registry.
```

### No-change exit

```
No requirement changes made. memory.md unchanged.
```

---

## Integration with Existing Commands

| Command | Behavior after iterate |
|---------|----------------------|
| `/kandalf:execute` | Picks up newly registered `pending` tasks and executes them |
| `/kandalf:resume` | Also picks up newly registered `pending` tasks (use after interrupted execution) |
| `/kandalf:status` | Shows iterate sessions in Session Log with outcome `iterate`; new tasks visible in Task Registry |
| `/kandalf:report` | Ignores iterate sessions (no `change` tasks executed in them); only execution sessions contribute to reports |
| `/kandalf:rollback` | Unaffected; can roll back completed tasks before or after iterate |
| `/kandalf:start` | If `memory.md` exists (even with `iterate` outcomes in Session Log), start correctly redirects to `/kandalf:execute` or `/kandalf:resume` |
| `/kandalf:update-docs` | Unaffected; doc sync reads current codebase state regardless of iterate sessions |

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/commands/01-start.md
==============================
---
title: "/kandalf:start"
tags: [kandalf, command, start, session]
aliases: [start command, kandalf start]
slug: commands/start
---

# /kandalf:start

> Run discovery Q&A, identify edge cases, and generate an approved task plan — ready for `/kandalf:execute`.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[commands/08-execute|execute]] · [[commands/07-iterate|iterate]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:start
  --docs <path>              Required. Documentation directory for your agent.
  --project <path>           Optional. Path to a project.md high-level description.
  --examples <dir>           Optional. Directory of conversation example files (any text format).
  --requirements <path>      Optional. Path to requirements or user-story document.
  --budget-threshold <pct>   Optional. Shutdown threshold % (default: 80, range: 1–99).
  --budget-limit <tokens>    Optional. Total token budget (default: 100000).
  --dry-run                  Optional. Preview mode: run discovery and register the
                             task plan, print it with a budget estimate, then stop
                             before executing anything.
```

---

## What It Does

`/kandalf:start` runs the discovery and planning pipeline, then stops — leaving execution to `/kandalf:execute`:

1. **Validates inputs** — checks `--docs` path exists and is readable
2. **Checks for existing memory.md** — if found and valid, stops and tells you to use `/kandalf:resume`; if found but corrupted, offers repair options
3. **Checks CodeGraph** — verifies `.codegraph/` exists in your project root; if missing, prompts you to run `codegraph install --yes` from your project root (outside the kandalf directory) before continuing; captures baseline metrics once the index is ready
4. **Ingests inputs** — reads all files in `--docs` (recursively), plus `--project`, `--examples`, `--requirements` if provided
5. **Discovery Q&A** — interactive session to define improvement goals, scope, success criteria, and constraints
6. **Edge case Q&A** — after scope is defined, analyzes the ingested docs for potential edge cases per in-scope component and surfaces them as explicit questions:
   - Groups edge cases by component area; presents up to 5 per area
   - Each question shows: `(a) Address it — describe desired behavior · (b) Out of scope — state your reason · (c) Already covered — which goal covers it`
   - Option (a) answers are written to `## Edge Case Coverage` in `memory.md` with disposition `addressed`; tasks are generated in Step 7
   - Option (b) answers are written to `## Edge Case Coverage` with disposition `dismissed` and also added to `## Open Questions` with your stated reason
   - Option (c) answers are written to `## Edge Case Coverage` with disposition `already-covered` and your reference
   - If no edge cases are found: outputs `No edge cases identified for [Component]. ✓` and continues
   - If budget is exhausted during Q&A: partial answers saved to `memory.md`; outputs `Q&A interrupted. Progress saved. Run /kandalf:start to resume.`
7. **Initializes memory.md** — writes session ID, project goal, scope, baseline CodeGraph metrics, config, and completed `## Edge Case Coverage` table to `memory.md`
8. **Decomposes tasks** — breaks improvement work into atomic tasks; also generates tasks for every edge case with disposition `addressed`; runs `codegraph_impact` on every `change` task; registers all tasks in `memory.md`; updates `## Edge Case Coverage` rows to replace `TBD` placeholders with assigned Task IDs; for edge cases with uncertain resolution, task Success Criteria is flagged `[needs-clarification]`
9. **Presents plan and stops** — displays the full task list with CodeGraph impact data, followed by the Edge Case Coverage Summary, then outputs:
   ```
   Plan ready. {N} tasks registered ({M} core + {K} edge case).

   Edge Case Coverage: {A} addressed (tasks: T-NNN, ...) · {D} dismissed · {C} already covered

   What would you like to do?
     → Run /kandalf:execute    — begin execution now
     → Run /kandalf:iterate    — refine requirements or add new tasks first
     → Run /kandalf:status     — review the full task registry
   ```
   **No tasks are executed by this command.** Execution begins only when you run `/kandalf:execute`.

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> `start` initializes the [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] (`knowledge/` by default) when absent — it is the only command allowed to create it — and selectively loads relevant entries at Section 4a (failures and constraints always). Task decomposition consults loaded failures before adopting any approach (`Prior failure on record: … Proceed anyway, adapt, or skip?`), each task completion captures a success entry, and session close writes the session's timeline section.

---

## Parameters

### `--docs <path>` *(required)*

Path to the documentation directory for the conversational agent you want to improve. All files are read recursively. Accepts any text format: Markdown, plain text, JSON, YAML, etc.

```
/kandalf:start --docs ./my-agent/docs
/kandalf:start --docs /absolute/path/to/docs
```

### `--project <path>` *(optional)*

A single file (typically `project.md`) providing a high-level description of your agent project. Gives kandalf broader context beyond the docs directory.

### `--examples <dir>` *(optional)*

Directory of conversation example files. Used to understand existing agent behavior patterns. Accepts any text format — copy-pasted conversations, JSON logs, plain text transcripts.

### `--requirements <path>` *(optional)*

A requirements document or user-story file that describes desired agent behavior. Used to align improvement tasks with stated goals.

### `--budget-threshold <pct>` *(optional, default: 80)*

Percentage of `--budget-limit` at which the session triggers a graceful shutdown. For example, `--budget-threshold 70` shuts down when 70% of the token budget is estimated to have been used.

Valid range: 1–99.

### `--dry-run` *(optional flag)*

Preview mode. Discovery Q&A and task decomposition run normally and the plan is
registered in `memory.md`, but nothing executes and no file outside `memory.md`
(and `.kandalf/`) changes. The session closes with outcome `completed` and a
reuse hint. Run the plan later with `/kandalf:execute` — discovery is not
repeated.

### `--budget-limit <tokens>` *(optional, default: 100000)*

Estimated total token budget for the improvement session. kandalf uses conservative upper-bound estimates per operation (see [[concepts/04-budget-detection|Budget Detection]]). Set higher for large projects with many change tasks.

---

## Prerequisites

- `memory.md` must NOT already exist at the project root (if it does and is valid, use `/kandalf:resume` instead)
- `--docs` path must be a readable directory
- CodeGraph is auto-installed if missing; Node.js 20+ required for `npx` install

---

## Session Output

On successful completion, `start` outputs the full task plan followed by:

```
Plan ready. {N} tasks registered ({M} core + {K} edge case).

Edge Case Coverage: {A} addressed (tasks: T-NNN, ...) · {D} dismissed · {C} already covered

What would you like to do?
  → Run /kandalf:execute    — begin execution now
  → Run /kandalf:iterate    — refine requirements or add new tasks first
  → Run /kandalf:status     — review the full task registry
```

If budget is exhausted during the Q&A or planning phase, partial progress is saved and you can resume by running `/kandalf:start` again (answered Q&A is preserved).

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: documentation directory not found at '<path>'. Provide a valid --docs path.` | `--docs` path does not exist or is not a directory | Check the path; use an absolute path if needed |
| `Error: --budget-threshold must be an integer between 1 and 99.` | Invalid `--budget-threshold` value | Use a value like `80` (no `%` symbol) |
| `Error: memory.md already exists. Run /kandalf:resume to continue, or /kandalf:status to inspect current state.` | A valid session is already in progress | Use `/kandalf:resume` or `/kandalf:rollback --all` then delete `memory.md` to start fresh |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted `memory.md` | Follow the offered repair options: reset to last checkpoint or archive and start fresh |
| `Warning: CodeGraph not initialized. Continuing without impact analysis.` | `.codegraph/` missing in project root | Run `codegraph install --yes` from your project root (outside the kandalf folder), then restart Claude Code |

---

## Examples

```
# Minimal — just docs directory
/kandalf:start --docs ./agent/docs

# With project description and conversation examples
/kandalf:start --docs ./agent/docs --project ./agent/project.md --examples ./agent/convos

# Full options — conservative budget for a large project
/kandalf:start \
  --docs ./agent/docs \
  --project ./agent/project.md \
  --examples ./agent/convos \
  --requirements ./agent/requirements.md \
  --budget-limit 200000 \
  --budget-threshold 75
```

---

## State Created

After a successful `start`, the following files exist:

```
memory.md                          # Config, Project Goal, Scope, Task Registry,
                                   # Task Details, Edge Case Coverage, Open Questions,
                                   # Session Log (start entry), empty Change Log,
                                   # empty Checkpoint Registry
knowledge/                         # Knowledge registry (index.md, timeline.md, entries/)
                                   # — created only if absent; see [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]]
.kandalf/
  sessions/                        # Directory created; session trace written on execute
```

**Not created by start** (created by `/kandalf:execute`):
- `.kandalf/checkpoints/` — checkpoint JSON files
- `.kandalf/rollbacks/` — original file copies before each change
- `.kandalf/tmp/` — staging area

==============================

title: "/kandalf:status"
tags: [kandalf, command, status]

---

# /kandalf:status

> Show the Task Registry and session summary for the current improvement session.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[commands/05-report|report]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:status
  --verbose    Optional. Show full Task Details block for each task.
```

---

## What It Does

1. Checks that `memory.md` exists and passes all 5 validation rules
2. Reads Task Registry, Session Log (last 3 entries), and Budget Tracking table from `memory.md`
3. Runs `codegraph status` to get index health, symbol count, and last sync time
4. Formats and outputs the status dashboard

---

## Output Format

```
kandalf — Session S-20260521-093045 | Budget: 42% used (42000/100000 est. tokens)

Project Goal: Improve the customer support agent's tone and tool-use accuracy.

Task Registry:
  ✅ T-001  Analyze system prompt tone issues              [analysis]  S-20260521-093045  —
  ✅ T-002  Analyze tool call patterns                     [analysis]  S-20260521-093045  —
  🔄 T-003  Rewrite system prompt tone section            [change]    S-20260521-093045  (step 2)
  ⬜ T-004  Update tool call instructions                  [change]    —                  —
  ⬜ T-005  Generate improvement report                    [report]    —                  —

Last session: S-20260521-093045 — active (started 2026-05-21T09:30:45Z)
CodeGraph: ✅ healthy | 847 symbols | last sync 3 minutes ago
```

### Status Icons

| Status | Icon | Meaning |
|--------|------|---------|
| `completed` | ✅ | Done and verified |
| `in_progress` | 🔄 | Currently executing |
| `interrupted` | ⏸ | Paused (budget or session end); resumable |
| `pending` | ⬜ | Not yet started |
| `blocked` | 🚫 | Waiting on a dependency |
| `deferred` | 💤 | Intentionally postponed |

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> The dashboard includes a Knowledge stats line summarizing the [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] (entry counts by type and status). If the registry is absent, the line notes that `/kandalf:start` will create it.

---

## Parameters

### `--verbose` *(optional flag)*

Show the full Task Details subsection from `memory.md` for each task, including: type, inputs, outputs, success criteria, effort estimate, CodeGraph impact, checkpoint ID, and resume instructions.

```
/kandalf:status --verbose
```

---

## Prerequisites

- `memory.md` must exist at the project root (created by `/kandalf:start`)

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start to begin.` | No active session | Run `/kandalf:start` |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted state | Follow repair options |
| `CodeGraph: ⚠ not initialized` | CodeGraph index missing | Run `codegraph install --yes` from your project root (outside the kandalf folder) |

---

## Examples

```
# Quick check — current task states and budget usage
/kandalf:status

# Verbose — full task details including checkpoint IDs
/kandalf:status --verbose
```

📂 FILE: ./docs/commands/05-report.md

---
title: "/kandalf:report"
tags: [kandalf, command, report, output]
aliases: [report command, kandalf report, improvement report]
slug: commands/report
---

# /kandalf:report

> Generate a structured before/after improvement report for all completed change tasks.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|CodeGraph Integration]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:report
  --format markdown|text    Optional. Output format. Default: markdown.
  --output <path>           Optional. Write report to file instead of printing to screen.
```

---

## What It Does

1. Reads and validates `memory.md`
2. Collects all tasks with type=`change` and status=`completed` — if none found, outputs an error and stops
3. Runs `codegraph status` for current index metrics
4. Reads baseline CodeGraph metrics from `memory.md` Config section
5. For each completed `change` task: reads the rollback copy (before) and current file (after); generates before/after snippet and test example
6. Assembles and outputs the full report

> [!note] Knowledge Registry
> Report generation draws on [[concepts/06-knowledge-registry|Knowledge Registry]] context — recorded decisions, rationale, and failures enrich the report narrative — and updates the current session's section in `timeline.md` (idempotent: updated in place if it already exists, never duplicated).

---

## Parameters

### `--format markdown|text` *(optional, default: markdown)*

Output format for the report. `markdown` produces fully formatted headers, bold text, and tables. `text` strips Markdown syntax for pasting into plain-text contexts.

```
/kandalf:report --format text
```

### `--output <path>` *(optional)*

Write the report to a file instead of printing to screen. The file is written atomically (via `.kandalf/tmp/report.tmp` → rename). If the write fails, an error is printed and the report is output to screen as a fallback.

```
/kandalf:report --output ./improvement-report.md
/kandalf:report --format text --output ./improvement-report.txt
```

---

## Prerequisites

- `memory.md` must exist at the project root (created by `/kandalf:start`)
- At least one `change` task must have status `completed`; analysis-only sessions produce a warning instead

---

## Report Structure

```markdown
# kandalf Improvement Report

**Project**: <first sentence of Project Goal>
**Sessions**: N | **Tasks Completed**: N | **Files Modified**: N
**Generated**: 2026-05-21T10:15:00Z

---

## Baseline Assessment
...

---

## Changes Applied
### T-001: <title>
...

---

## Delta Summary
| Metric | Before | After | Change |
...

---

## Recommended Next Steps
...
```

---

## Sections

### Baseline Assessment

Compares CodeGraph metrics at session start (from `memory.md` Config) vs. current:
- Symbols indexed at session start
- Current symbol count
- CodeGraph backend (native / wasm)
- Issues identified during discovery

### Changes Applied

One subsection per completed `change` task:

```markdown
### T-003: Rewrite system prompt tone section

**What changed**: prompts/system.md — rewrote tone guidance paragraphs
**Rationale**: Agent was using overly formal language; discovery Q&A identified this as the top improvement goal
**Impact radius** (CodeGraph): Affects 4 symbols across 2 files: systemPrompt (prompts/system.md), toneConfig (config/agent.json)

**Before**:
> [first 30 meaningful lines of original]

**After**:
> [first 30 meaningful lines of current file]

**Test example**:
- Input: "Hi, can you help me reset my password?"
- Previous behavior: "Greetings. I shall assist you with the password reset procedure."
- New behavior: "Of course! Let's get your password reset right away."
- How to validate: Send the input above in a new conversation and compare the opening sentence style
```

If the rollback copy is unavailable: `Warning: original content for T-003 not available — before/after comparison omitted for this task.`

### Delta Summary

Markdown table comparing before/after across key metrics:

| Metric | Before | After | Change |
|--------|--------|-------|--------|
| Symbols indexed | 847 | 851 | +4 |
| Files modified | 0 | 2 | +2 |
| Tasks completed | 0 | 3 | +3 |

### Recommended Next Steps

Lists tasks with status `pending` or `deferred`. If all tasks are completed: `All improvement tasks completed.`

---

## Output Options

### Default (stdout, markdown)

Report is printed directly to the Claude Code conversation.

### `--format text`

Strips Markdown syntax (headings, bold, table delimiters) and outputs plain text.

### `--output <path>`

Writes the report to the specified file atomically (via `.kandalf/tmp/report.tmp` → rename). If the write fails: `Error: cannot write to '<path>'. Check permissions.`

On success, outputs: `Report written to <path>`

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Resolution |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `No completed changes found. Run /kandalf:status to see pending tasks, or /kandalf:resume to continue working.` | No `change` tasks with status `completed` | Complete at least one change task first |
| `Error: memory.md not found. Run /kandalf:start first.` | No active session | Run `/kandalf:start` |
| `Error: cannot write to '<path>'. Check permissions.` | Output path not writable | Check file permissions or use a different `--output` path |

---

## Examples

```
# Print report to screen
/kandalf:report

# Save as Markdown file
/kandalf:report --output ./improvement-report.md

# Plain text for pasting into a ticket
/kandalf:report --format text --output ./improvement-report.txt
```

==============================
📂 FILE: ./docs/commands/08-execute.md
==============================
---
title: "/kandalf:execute"
tags: [kandalf, command, execute, session]
aliases: [execute command, kandalf execute]
slug: commands/execute
---

# /kandalf:execute

> Execute the approved task plan from `/kandalf:start`.

See also: [[04-workflow|Workflow Guide]] · [[concepts/01-memory-schema|memory.md Schema]] · [[commands/01-start|start]] · [[commands/02-resume|resume]] · [[commands/07-iterate|iterate]] · [[commands/03-status|status]] · [[07-troubleshooting|Troubleshooting]]

---

## Syntax

```
/kandalf:execute
  --budget-threshold <pct>   Optional. Override shutdown threshold for this session.
                             Valid range: 1–99. Default: value from memory.md Config.
```

---

## What It Does

`/kandalf:execute` runs the task plan produced by `/kandalf:start`:

1. **Validates memory.md** — same 5 rules as `/kandalf:start`. Halts with repair options on failure.

2. **Checks for interrupted tasks** — scans Task Registry for any tasks with status `interrupted` or stale `in_progress`. If found:
   ```
   ⚠️  {N} interrupted task(s) detected:
     T-001 — {title} (interrupted at checkpoint T-001-step2-S-...)
     ...

   Interrupted tasks must be resolved before execution continues.

   What would you like to do?
     → Run /kandalf:resume   — resolve interrupted tasks, then continue
     → Run /kandalf:status   — review full task state before deciding
     → Run /kandalf:rollback T-NNN   — undo a specific interrupted task
   ```
   The command exits after showing this message. It does not attempt to execute `pending` tasks alongside interrupted ones.

3. **Checks for locked (in_progress) tasks** — if any tasks are owned by another active session:
   ```
   ⚠️  Warning: {N} task(s) currently in_progress (owner: {Session ID}).
   Tasks: T-{NNN} — {title}, ...

   These tasks may be actively running in another session.
   Continue anyway? (yes / no)
     yes — proceed (locked tasks treated as immutable; only pending tasks executed)
     no  — exit; check /kandalf:status for details
   ```

4. **Checks for no pending tasks** — if all tasks are already `completed` or `deferred`:
   ```
   ✓  All tasks are already completed (or deferred).

   What would you like to do next?
     → Run /kandalf:report       — generate improvement report
     → Run /kandalf:iterate      — add new requirements
     → Run /kandalf:status       — review task registry
     → Run /kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs   — sync documentation
   ```
   The command exits cleanly without error.

5. **Displays execution plan** — shows pending tasks grouped by execution wave (tasks that can run in parallel in the same wave):
   ```
   Executing {N} pending tasks across {W} waves.

   Wave 1 (parallel): T-001, T-002
   Wave 2: T-003 (depends on T-001)
   Wave 3: T-004 (depends on T-003)

   Starting execution...
   ```

6. **Executes tasks in dependency order** — for each task:
   - Status → `in_progress`, Owner → current session ID
   - Sub-agent spawned (or inline for small analysis tasks)
   - Sub-agent follows the [[concepts/03-codegraph-integration|10-step CodeGraph data flow]] for `change` tasks
   - Sub-agent follows the [[concepts/02-checkpoint-protocol|6-step checkpoint protocol]] for every file write
   - After completion: task verified against its Success Criteria
   - If pass → status `completed`, brief completion note shown
   - If fail → status stays `in_progress` with retry note; execution continues with independent tasks

7. **Runs parallelizable tasks concurrently** — tasks with no overlapping output files run via concurrent sub-agents. Each sub-agent reads `memory.md` to check file ownership before touching any file.

8. **Monitors budget after each task** — checks estimated remaining tokens against `budget_threshold_pct`. Approaching threshold triggers graceful shutdown (see Graceful Shutdown below).

9. **Runs CodeGraph impact analysis** for every `change` task before first file write. Tasks with impact radius > 20 symbols trigger a confirmation prompt:
   ```
   High-impact change: T-{NNN} affects {N} symbols across {N} files.
   Continue with this task? (yes / no / defer)
     yes   — proceed
     no    — skip this task (remains pending)
     defer — set task to deferred, continue with remaining tasks
   ```

10. **Reports progress inline** — after each task completes:
    ```
    ✓ T-{NNN} — {title} [type] ({effort})
    ```
    After each wave:
    ```
    Wave {N} complete. {N} tasks done, {N} remaining.
    ```

11. **Closes session on completion** — writes Session Log entry, outputs summary:
    ```
    Execution session S-{ID} complete.
    Tasks completed : {N}
    Tasks remaining : {N} (deferred or skipped)
    Budget used     : ~{N} tokens

    What would you like to do next?
      → Run /kandalf:report                      — generate improvement report
      → Run /kandalf:iterate                     — refine requirements and add new tasks
      → Run /kandalf:update-docs --docs ./docs   — sync documentation
      → Run /kandalf:status                      — review full task registry
    ```

---

## Parameters

### `--budget-threshold <pct>` *(optional)*

Override the shutdown threshold for this execute session. Valid range: 1–99. Default: the `budget_threshold_pct` stored in `memory.md` Config.

```
/kandalf:execute --budget-threshold 60
```

---

## Graceful Shutdown

If the budget threshold is reached during execution:

1. The current atomic task step completes (never abandoned mid-write)
2. In-progress tasks get a checkpoint saved
3. Task status → `interrupted`; Owner cleared
4. Session Log → End Time + outcome (`interrupted-budget` or `interrupted-quota`)
5. Output:
   ```
   Budget threshold reached. Saving state...

   Progress saved. Session S-{ID} interrupted.
   Completed this session : {N} tasks
   Interrupted            : {N} tasks
   Still pending          : {N} tasks

   What would you like to do?
     → Run /kandalf:resume   — continue from where you left off
     → Run /kandalf:status   — review what was done and what remains
     → Run /kandalf:report   — generate partial report on completed tasks
   ```

---

## Prerequisites

- Plugin loaded: `claude --plugin-dir ./kandalf`
- `memory.md` must exist at the project root (created by `/kandalf:start`)
- At least one task must have status `pending` (or the command reports all-complete and exits)

---

## Error Messages

| Error | Cause | What to do |
|-------|-------|-----------|
| `Error: memory.md not found.` | No session in progress | Run `/kandalf:start --docs <path>` to begin |
| `memory.md validation failed: Rule N — ...` | Corrupted state | Follow repair options: reset to checkpoint or archive and start fresh |
| `Error: --budget-threshold must be an integer between 1 and 99.` | Invalid parameter | Use a value like `80` (no `%` symbol) |

---

## Examples

```
# Run the task plan produced by start
/kandalf:execute

# Run with a tighter budget threshold
/kandalf:execute --budget-threshold 60

# Typical full workflow
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs        # Discovery Q&A + plan
/kandalf:execute                    # Execute the plan

# Workflow with requirement refinement
/kandalf:start --docs ./docs        # Discovery Q&A + plan
/kandalf:iterate                    # Refine requirements
/kandalf:execute                    # Execute updated plan
```

---

## State Created / Modified

`/kandalf:execute` modifies `memory.md` and creates runtime files:

| What | Change |
|------|--------|
| Task Registry | Status transitions: `pending` → `in_progress` → `completed` (or `interrupted`) |
| Change Log | `planned` → `applied` entries per file modified |
| Session Log | New row added with outcome `completed`, `interrupted-budget`, `interrupted-quota`, or `interrupted-user` |
| Checkpoint Registry | Rows added per checkpoint created during execution |
| `.kandalf/rollbacks/{taskId}/` | Original file copies created before each `change` task |
| `.kandalf/checkpoints/` | Checkpoint JSON files created during execution |
| `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json` | Full session trace written on completion or interruption |

**Does not modify**:
- `## Project Goal`, `## Scope`, `## Config`, `## Open Questions`, `## Edge Case Coverage`
- Any task with status `completed` (already done — execute skips these)

---

## Integration with Other Commands

| Command | Relationship |
|---------|-------------|
| `/kandalf:start` | Produces the plan that `execute` runs — always run `start` before `execute` |
| `/kandalf:iterate` | Can be run between `start` and `execute` to refine requirements; `execute` will pick up the updated task registry |
| `/kandalf:resume` | Handles interrupted sessions; if `execute` detects interrupted tasks it redirects here |
| `/kandalf:status` | Shows what `execute` has done and what remains |
| `/kandalf:rollback` | Undoes completed-task changes; rolled-back tasks reset to `pending` and become eligible for `execute` again |
| `/kandalf:report` | Run after `execute` to generate the before/after improvement report |
| `/kandalf:update-docs` | Run after `execute` to sync documentation with changed codebase |

==============================

# Hook: on-workspace-load

**Trigger:** Automatically runs when Antigravity CLI initializes in a directory containing `CLAUDE.md` or `memory.md`.

**Instructions:**
You are Kandalf, an Agent Engineering Harness. 
A Kandalf project environment has been detected. You MUST automatically:
1. Read the local `CLAUDE.md` file and adopt its execution rules as your core persona.
2. Read the local `memory.md` file to establish the current project timeline, active task, and state.
3. Inform the user that the Kandalf harness is active and summarize the current active task from memory.

Do not wait for the user to ask you to do this. Do it immediately upon load.

📂 FILE: ./lib/codegraph-integration.md

# codegraph-integration: Mandatory CodeGraph Usage Rules for Sub-Agents

Every sub-agent spawned by kandalf MUST follow these rules. No code change or
file modification may proceed without first querying CodeGraph.

---

## Prerequisite: CodeGraph Must Be Initialized

Before any sub-agent begins work, verify `.codegraph/` exists in the project root.

If `.codegraph/` is absent:
- Run `codegraph init -i` (or alert the parent session to do so).
- Do not modify any files until the index is built.

---

## Available MCP Tools

| Tool | Purpose | When to Use |
|------|---------|-------------|
| `codegraph_search` | Find symbols by name across the codebase | First step when locating a target function, class, or file |
| `codegraph_context` | Build relevant code context for a task | During `analysis` tasks to understand a component's role |
| `codegraph_impact` | Analyze what code is affected by changing a symbol | **MANDATORY** before any file write in a `change` task |
| `codegraph_callers` | Find what calls a function | Understanding dependencies before refactoring |
| `codegraph_callees` | Find what a function calls | Understanding a function's reach before changing it |
| `codegraph_node` | Get details about a specific symbol | Inspecting a single symbol's definition and source |
| `codegraph_files` | Get indexed file structure | Faster than filesystem scanning for project layout |
| `codegraph_status` | Check index health and statistics | Session start baseline + report generation |
| `codegraph_affected` | Find test files affected by changed source files | After every file write to identify tests to run |

---

## 10-Step Data Flow for Change Tasks

Every `change` task sub-agent MUST execute these steps in order.

**Step 0 — documentation lookup (when tagged)**: if the task involves an external
library, framework, SDK, or CLI tool API, run the lookup operation from
`lib/context7-protocol.md` BEFORE step 1 — current docs must inform the change
before any analysis is logged or any file is written. Tasks with no external
dependency skip this step entirely (zero added latency).

```
1. codegraph_search("{target symbol or filename}")
   → Locate the exact symbol or file to modify
   → Confirm it is the intended target (not a similarly-named symbol)

2. codegraph_impact("{symbol}", depth=3)
   → Blast-radius analysis: what calls this, what this calls, what imports it
   → HALT if impact radius is unexpectedly large (>20 symbols): alert parent session
     and request task split or explicit user confirmation before proceeding

3. Log impact summary to memory.md Task Details, CodeGraph Impact field:
   "Affects N symbols across M files: [symbol1 (file.md), ...]"
   → This is required BEFORE writing any rollback copy or checkpoint

4. codegraph_callers("{symbol}")
   → Understand all upstream dependencies that will be affected

5. Copy original file to rollbacks/{taskId}/  [Step 1 of checkpoint-protocol.md]

6. Write planned entry to Change Log          [Step 2 of checkpoint-protocol.md]

7. Write checkpoint JSON                      [Step 3 of checkpoint-protocol.md]

8. Write new content to tmp/{file}.tmp        [Step 4 of checkpoint-protocol.md]

9. Rename tmp → target path                   [Step 5 of checkpoint-protocol.md]

10. codegraph_affected([modified files])
    → Identify test files that must be re-run after the change
    → Log affected test files in task Notes field
    → Update Change Log entry to applied     [Step 6 of checkpoint-protocol.md]
```

---

## Impact Radius Thresholds

| Impact Radius | Action |
|---------------|--------|
| 0–5 symbols | Proceed normally |
| 6–20 symbols | Note all affected symbols in CodeGraph Impact field; proceed |
| 21+ symbols | HALT; alert parent session; request task split or explicit confirmation |
| Cross-component impact | Note boundary being crossed; proceed only after confirmation |

---

## Rules for Analysis Tasks (type: analysis)

`analysis` tasks do NOT modify files. They MUST:
1. Use `codegraph_context` and `codegraph_search` instead of grep/glob/Read for exploration.
2. Use `codegraph_node` to inspect specific symbols.
3. Write findings to memory.md task Notes field.
4. NOT call `codegraph_impact` unless specifically analyzing impact for a planned future change.

---

## Rules for Sub-Agent File Ownership

Before a sub-agent begins work on any file, it MUST:
1. Read the Task Registry in `memory.md`.
2. Check if any other task has status `in_progress` and lists that file in its Outputs.
3. If yes: HALT. Do not modify the file. Alert parent session — file is owned by another
   in-progress task.
4. If no: proceed.

A sub-agent MUST NOT modify any file outside its task's declared `Outputs` list.

---

## Logging Requirements

After every `codegraph_impact` call, the sub-agent MUST log in the task's CodeGraph
Impact field in memory.md:

```
[{timestamp}] codegraph_impact("{symbol}"): {N} symbols affected across {M} files.
Affected: {symbol1} ({file1.ext}), {symbol2} ({file2.ext}), ...
Test files to run: {test1.ext}, {test2.ext}
```

This log is used by `/kandalf report` to generate quantitative justification
for each change.

---

## CodeGraph Auto-Sync

CodeGraph auto-syncs on file save (2-second debounce). After any batch of file changes:
1. Wait ~3 seconds for auto-sync to complete.
2. Run `codegraph status` to confirm index is fresh.
3. If `codegraph status` shows stale index: run `codegraph sync` manually.

After rollback operations, always run `codegraph sync` explicitly to rebuild from
the restored file state.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./lib/knowledge-protocol.md
==============================
# knowledge-protocol: Cross-Session Knowledge Registry Operations

The knowledge registry is the project's durable memory across sessions: what worked,
what didn't, and why. It lives in the TARGET project at `{knowledge_path}` (memory.md
Config, default `knowledge/`) and is never archived — unlike session state in
`memory.md`, registry entries persist for the life of the project.

Every registry read or write MUST go through the operations below. Commands MUST NOT
manipulate registry files ad hoc.

Contract: `specs/015-knowledge-registry/contracts/knowledge-protocol-contract.md` ·
Tests: `tests/contract/test-knowledge-contract.md`

---

## Registry Layout

```
{knowledge_path}/
├── index.md                  # entries grouped by type (template: knowledge-index-template.md)
├── timeline.md               # reverse-chronological session sections
└── entries/
    └── K-NNN-short-slug.md   # one note per entry (template: knowledge-entry-template.md)
```

**Entry frontmatter** — required: `id` (K-NNN), `title`, `type` (`decision` |
`success` | `failure` | `constraint` | `insight`), `status` (`active` |
`superseded`), `created` (ISO 8601), `session` (S-ID or `manual`), `tags`
(always includes `knowledge-registry`). Optional: `task`, `supersedes`,
`superseded_by`.

**K-NNN allocation**: highest existing ID across `entries/` filenames and
frontmatter, +1, zero-padded 3 digits (extend to 4 past 999) — same convention
as Task IDs in `lib/common-preamble.md`.

## Obsidian Conventions

Write all registry notes as Obsidian Flavored Markdown. The `obsidian-markdown`
skill is BUNDLED with the plugin (`kandalf/skills/obsidian-markdown/`, vendored
from kepano/obsidian-skills, MIT) — follow it for syntax details. The summary
below states the same conventions for quick reference:

- **Properties**: YAML frontmatter between `---` fences at the very top; lists in
  `[a, b]` form; quote values containing `[[` (e.g., `supersedes: "[[K-006-slug]]"`).
- **Wikilinks**: `[[filename-without-extension]]` or `[[file|display text]]`;
  escape `|` as `\|` inside tables.
- **Callouts**: `> [!warning] Title` followed by `> ` body lines.

---

## Operations

### OP-1 — initialize

**Caller**: start (only command allowed to create the registry).

When `{knowledge_path}` is absent or empty: create the directory and `entries/`,
write `index.md` and `timeline.md` from `templates/knowledge-index-template.md`,
record `knowledge_path` in memory.md Config, and print exactly:

```
Knowledge registry initialized at '{knowledge_path}'.
```

Then proceed with normal discovery — there is no prior knowledge to cite.

### OP-2 — selective-load

**Callers**: start (after docs ingestion), resume (after memory.md validation).

Read, in order — and nothing else at startup:

1. `index.md` in full.
2. The 3 most recent session sections of `timeline.md`.
3. Entry notes where `status: active` AND (`type` is `failure` or `constraint`,
   OR frontmatter `tags` overlap keywords extracted from the stated session goal).

All other entries load lazily when a topic match arises (OP-3). This keeps startup
cost flat as the registry grows. Print exactly:

```
Knowledge registry: {N} entries loaded ({F} failures, {C} constraints, {M} goal-matched) of {T} total.
```

Count each loaded entry once, in the first matching category (failure > constraint
> goal-matched). `{T}` counts parseable entries only.

**Tolerance** (applies to every operation that reads entries):
- Unparseable entry (broken frontmatter, missing required field):

  ```
  Warning: knowledge entry '{relative/path}' could not be parsed ({reason}). Entry skipped; fix or remove it.
  ```

  One warning per file per session. Never abort.
- Duplicate `id`: use the newest by `created`; print:

  ```
  Warning: duplicate knowledge ID '{id}' — using newest by created date.
  ```

- Missing optional metadata: accept silently.
- Registry absent and caller is not start: print a one-line note that the registry
  is missing and that `/kandalf:start` will create it; continue as a no-op.

### OP-3 — consult

**Callers**: start (task decomposition), resume (interrupted-task resolution).

Before adopting any proposed approach, compare it against loaded `failure` entries
(same component, same technique, or same file set). On overlap, present — and do
NOT silently adopt the approach:

```
Prior failure on record: [[{K-NNN-slug}]] — {title} ({session}). Reason: {one-line rationale}. Proceed anyway, adapt, or skip?
```

`constraint` entries are binding context: surface any constraint the approach
would violate. When decomposition raises a topic not in the loaded set, lazily
load active entries whose tags match before finalizing that task.

In resume, recorded failures and retry conditions inform the resume-vs-rollback
recommendation for interrupted tasks (cite the entry when they change the advice).

### OP-4 — capture-success

**Callers**: start (task completion — checkpoint protocol Step 6), update-docs
(doc task completion).

When a task completes and validates:

1. Allocate the next K-NNN; create `entries/K-NNN-{slug}.md` from
   `templates/knowledge-entry-template.md`: `type: success`, what changed
   (Description), why it worked (Rationale), files touched + CodeGraph impact
   summary (Context), `session`/`task` refs.
2. Run the OP-7 supersession check.
3. Update `index.md` in the same operation (Registry Dual-Update Rule below).

The entry MUST be on disk before the next task begins — capture is incremental;
an interrupted session loses at most the in-flight step (CT-KNOW-PERSIST-01).

Routine completions yield one concise entry; do not pad. Skip capture entirely
only when a task taught nothing durable (e.g., a no-op validation), and note the
skip in the task's Notes field.

### OP-5 — capture-failure

**Callers**: rollback (after rollback completes), start (task abandoned or failed).

Two cases:

- **No entry exists for the task**: create one — `type: failure`, the attempted
  approach (Description), why it failed (Rationale), and `## Retry Conditions`
  (conditions under which the approach may be retried; "none known" is valid).
- **A success entry exists for the task** (rollback after capture): rewrite that
  SAME note in place — flip `type` to `failure`, keep `id` and filename, append:

  ```
  > [!warning] Rolled back
  > Rolled back in {sessionId} ({timestamp}): {reason}.
  ```

  Do not create a second entry.

Then update `index.md` (regroup if the type changed) and append a reversal line to
the current session's `timeline.md` section.

### OP-6 — capture-manual

**Callers**: any command, when the user states a learning to record.

Create the entry immediately — `type` as stated (default `insight`), `session` =
current session ID (or `manual` outside a session), omit `task` unless stated —
update `index.md`, confirm in one line, and return to the task in progress
without further discussion:

```
Recorded [[{K-NNN-slug}]] ({type}): {title}.
```

### OP-7 — supersede

**Caller**: any capture operation whose new learning contradicts an active entry.

Set on the new entry: `supersedes: "[[{old-slug}]]"`. Set on the old entry:
`status: superseded` and `superseded_by: "[[{new-slug}]]"`. Update both index rows.
NEVER delete the old note — contradicted knowledge is history, not garbage.
When loading, prefer the newest `active` entry on any topic.

### OP-8 — session-summary

**Callers**: start/resume (graceful shutdown, alongside the session handoff),
report (report generation).

Prepend (newest first) a section to `timeline.md`:

```
## {sessionId} — {date}

Goal: {one-line goal}. Outcome: {Session Log outcome value}.

- [[{K-NNN-slug}]] — {one-line gloss}   (one line per entry captured this session)

Reversal: {task ID and reason}           (only if a rollback occurred this session)
```

**Idempotent per session**: if a section for this session ID already exists,
update it in place — never duplicate it (CT-KNOW-SUMMARY-02).

### OP-9 — docs-sync

**Caller**: update-docs.

- **At run start**: read entries with `created` newer than Config
  `last_knowledge_docs_sync` (value `never` means all entries). Feed their
  decisions, rationale, and evolution notes into the documentation updates —
  this is the knowledge that exists nowhere in the codebase.
- **On successful completion only**: set `last_knowledge_docs_sync` to the run
  timestamp in memory.md Config. An interrupted run leaves it untouched
  (CT-KNOW-DOCSYNC-02), so the next run re-reads the window.

---

## Registry Dual-Update Rule

The entry note and its `index.md` row are two views of one record (extension of
the Dual-Update Convention in `lib/common-preamble.md`). Every capture or
supersession writes both in the same operation; OP-5 and OP-8 additionally write
`timeline.md`. Never let them diverge.

## Invariants

1. The harness never deletes a registry file.
2. Registry problems never abort a session — worst case is a warning and degraded
   knowledge context.
3. Only start creates the registry (OP-1); every other caller no-ops gracefully
   when it is absent.
4. Durable learnings live in the registry; session-scoped working data stays in
   `memory.md`. Do not duplicate one into the other.
5. All notes are valid Obsidian Flavored Markdown per the conventions above.

==============================
📂 FILE: ./lib/budget-detection.md
==============================
# budget-detection: Token Budget Estimation and Graceful Shutdown Algorithm

kandalf does not have privileged access to the Claude API token counter.
Budget is estimated via a running context-size tracker.

---

## Configuration

Budget settings live in `memory.md` Config section:

| Field | Default | Override |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `budget_limit` | 100000 | Set during `/kandalf start` discovery or via `--budget-limit` |
| `budget_threshold_pct` | 80 | Per-session via `--budget-threshold <pct>` flag or by editing `memory.md` Config |

**Threshold computation**: `shutdown_threshold = budget_limit × (budget_threshold_pct / 100)`

Example: `budget_limit=100000`, `budget_threshold_pct=80` → shutdown at 80,000 estimated tokens.

---

## Estimation Algorithm

The parent session maintains a running token estimate:

```
tokens_estimated = 0

On each operation:
  tokens_estimated += estimate_tokens(operation)
```

**Operation cost estimates** (conservative approximations):

| Operation | Estimated Tokens |
|-----------|-----------------|
| Read `memory.md` (full) | 2,000–8,000 (scale with file size) |
| Write checkpoint JSON (small task) | 500–2,000 |
| Sub-agent spawn (overhead) | 1,000 |
| Sub-agent analysis task | 5,000–20,000 |
| Sub-agent change task (small file) | 3,000–10,000 |
| Sub-agent change task (large file) | 10,000–30,000 |
| codegraph_impact query | 200–500 |
| codegraph_context query | 1,000–5,000 |
| User interaction round-trip | 500–2,000 |
| Session Handoff Summary generation | 1,000–3,000 |

Use the UPPER bound of each range for conservative estimation.

---

## Threshold Check (run before each operation)

Before spawning a sub-agent or starting a new task step:

```
remaining = budget_limit - tokens_estimated
if tokens_estimated >= shutdown_threshold:
    TRIGGER GRACEFUL SHUTDOWN
```

If the NEXT planned operation would push over threshold:
- Complete the current atomic step if already in progress.
- Then trigger graceful shutdown.

**Do NOT start a new task if `tokens_estimated + expected_task_cost > shutdown_threshold`.**
Instead, include the task in Session Handoff Summary as "not started."

---

## Graceful Shutdown Sequence (mandatory order)

When threshold is crossed OR approaching:

1. **Complete the current atomic step** — never abandon mid-step. If a sub-agent is
   mid-checkpoint-protocol, let it finish steps 4–6 of checkpoint-protocol.md.

2. **Write checkpoint** — ensure the current task has a valid checkpoint at its current
   step (run Steps 3–6 of checkpoint-protocol.md if not already done).

3. **Update task status** — set the in-progress task to `interrupted` in both Task
   Registry and Task Details. Clear `owner`.

4. **Write session end to Session Log** — update End Time and Outcome:
   - Budget hit: `interrupted-budget`
   - Quota hit: `interrupted-quota`
   - User requested: `interrupted-user`

5. **Update Budget Tracking table** — append row with tokens estimated and outcome.

6. **Generate Session Handoff Summary** — use `templates/session-handoff-template.md`.
   Fill: tasks completed, tasks interrupted (with last checkpoint ID and next action),
   tasks not started, budget status at close, recommended next session scope.

7. **Append Session Handoff Summary to `memory.md`** — append after the Open Questions
   section. This makes it visible in the next session's memory read.

8. **Write session JSON** — write `.kandalf/sessions/{sessionId}.json` with full
   execution trace.

9. **Output the Session Handoff Summary** — print it as the final output before ending.

---

## Recommended Next Session Scope (step 6 calculation)

To compute recommended scope for the next session:

```
remaining_budget = budget_limit  # Fresh session starts with full budget
safety_margin = budget_limit × 0.15  # Reserve 15% for overhead and shutdown
usable = budget_limit - safety_margin

for each pending task (in dependency order):
    cost = upper_bound_estimate(task.estimated_effort)
    if usable - cost >= 0:
        include task in recommendation
        usable -= cost
    else:
        stop
```

Effort → cost mapping:
- `small` → 15,000 tokens
- `medium` → 35,000 tokens
- `large` → 70,000 tokens

---

## Budget Tracking Table Entry Format

```
| {sessionId} | {tokens_estimated} | {budget_limit} | {interrupted: yes/no} | {resumed_by: sessionId or —} |
```

`Interrupted` is `yes` for any outcome other than `completed`.
`Resumed By` is filled in by the NEXT session when it resumes this session's tasks.
